===== .changeset/r19-drafting.md =====

R19: drafting at `spec` and `test-design`. A run admitted with a person's
intent and no spec, or with no acceptance tests, drafts them on the line: a
drafter writes, the runtime records the bytes it read under one hash, the base
run runs a test draft, an independent critic reads it, and the exit's approval
carries the drafted floor; what is approved is written into the artifact root
and the base and locked against that hash (D-A-DS-01 to D-A-DS-07, D-R19-01 to
D-R19-20, A-R19-01 to A-R19-10).

- core: `spec` and `test-design` are agent stations with drafter contracts;
  `CRITIC_CONTRACTS` holds the critic seats. `ContextGrant` gains `intent`,
  `draft`, `rubric`. `ReviewSeat.independence` is an `IndependenceGrade`
  (`different-family`, `different-model`, `same-model`). `effectiveApproval`
  takes the drafted floor; `ApprovalGrant` and the transition match a grant
  by key, draft hash, and attempt. `RunState.drafts` records each station's
  attempts. `ParkCause` gains `drafts-rejected`; `StationRefusal` gains
  `same-family-drafter`. New: `seatTask`, `seatTestDrafter`, `gradeOf`,
  `retrySuffix`, `grantedCriticContext`, `DRAFT_ROLES`.
- vault: `AdmissionRecord` holds each artifact as supplied or drafted, the
  acceptance manifest, the two new spec parts, the intent, and the rubrics.
  New operations `recordIntent`, `recordRubric`, `recordDraft`,
  `recordCriticResult`, `recordDraftLock`, `readLocks`. Base records are kept
  once per run and draft hash (`readBaseRun(runId, draft)`). New decisions
  `approval-rejected`, `draft-sent-back`, `draft-floor-applied`. A record or
  state written before R19 reads as it did.
- api: `RequestedArtifacts` takes `'draft'`, an acceptance manifest, and the
  run-requirements and questions parts; `RunRequest` takes `intent` and
  `rubrics`; `ComponentGraph.drafting` names each seat's driver.
  `approveStation` takes the draft hash; `rejectDraft` is new. A run's
  standing names the draft a person decides. The worst case counts every
  drafting seat. R2's rates gain grade keys. Default rubrics ship as
  `DEFAULT_RUBRICS_DIRECTORY`. HTTP: `POST /runs` takes `intent`,
  `POST /runs/:id/approve` takes `draft`, and `POST /runs/:id/reject` is new;
  the host reads `FACTORY_RUBRICS_DIR`.
- cli: `create` takes `--draft-spec`, `--intent`, `--draft-tests`,
  `--acceptance-manifest`, `--run-requirements`, and `--questions`;
  `approve` takes `--draft`; `reject` is new.
- compiler: ships the `spec-drafter`, `spec-critic`, `test-drafter`, and
  `test-critic` roles.
- conformance: one assertion for each of R19's conformance sentences, P14's
  table rows for the new decisions, and I1's operation set at twenty-four.

===== packages/api/rubrics/spec.md =====
# Rubric: a drafted spec

A person owns this file and edits it; the runtime reads it at admission and
gives it to the spec critic. Change it to hold specs to your own standard.

A spec passes only if every point below holds. Each point that does not is a
finding.

1. Every statement can be checked by a test that calls the public interface.
   Words like "fast", "robust", "clean", or "as appropriate" are not checkable.
2. The spec covers what the intent asks for. Each thing the intent asks for is
   stated.
3. The spec asks for nothing the intent did not. Extra features, refactors,
   and clean-ups are findings.
4. The security part names each trust boundary, kind of sensitive data,
   authorization rule, and secret the change touches, or says `none` with a
   reason that is true for this change.
5. Each point the intent left open is either an assumption with what was
   chosen, or an open question. A choice made silently in the spec is a
   finding.
6. The run requirements name a test command, a framework, a test directory
   the framework reads, and a public interface precise enough to call from a
   test without reading the code.

===== packages/api/rubrics/test-design.md =====
# Rubric: drafted acceptance tests

A person owns this file and edits it; the runtime reads it at admission and
gives it to the test critic. Change it to hold tests to your own standard.

The tests pass only if every point below holds. Each point that does not is a
finding.

1. Each test checks a statement of the spec, and the statement it checks can
   be named.
2. Each statement of the spec is checked by at least one test.
3. Each test calls only the public interface the run requirements name. A test
   that reaches into internals, private state, or file layout the spec does
   not state is not black-box.
4. Each authorization rule the security part names has a test in which a
   caller the rule refuses is refused, and that check names the rule.
5. Each check's `onBase` is honest: `fails` only where the behavior is absent
   before the change, `passes` only where it already holds.
6. No test passes whatever the code does: no assertion that cannot fail, no
   test that only checks a mock.

 /**
  * What a run cost, derived from its `UsageRecord`s and nothing else (D-P13-08).
  *
  * No total is stored anywhere: a stored total is a second figure that can
  * disagree with its parts, so every total here is recomputed from the records
  * each time it is asked for. The records are what the relay counted, read by
  * the runtime after each sandbox stopped (I2); what a driver reported it used
  * is in its `TaskResult` and is not an input here.
  *
  * A call whose sandbox had no relay is `unmetered`. Its cost is not zero, it
  * is unknown, so a total counts such calls separately and its figures cover
  * the metered calls alone. A call whose usage the relay could not read is
  * charged what it could count, and a call whose reading was lost is charged
  * nothing; both make a total a lower bound (D-P13-19, A-I1a-04). A `pending`
  * reading that no terminal reading followed is a call a process stop took,
  * and is counted as lost; one that was followed is not a call of its own
  * (D-I1a-12). `bound` says which a total is, so a consumer does not have to

   return { usd: Number(micro) / MICRO, calls: tasks.reduce((sum, t) => sum + t.calls, 0), tasks };
 }
 
 /**
  * `calls` times `usd`, in whole millionths of a dollar, rounded up, so a
  * task's share is never less than the ceiling it stands for. Computed exactly
  * from the number's shortest decimal spelling, the one the policy author
  * wrote, so `0.07` is 70000 and not the 70001 that `Math.ceil(0.07 * 1e6)`
  * gives from the binary value.
  */

===== packages/api/src/drafting.ts =====
/**
 * Drafting at `spec` and `test-design` (R19). A run admitted without its spec
 * or its acceptance tests drafts them on the line: a drafter writes, the
 * runtime reads exactly the draft's paths and records the bytes it read under
 * one hash, the base run runs a test draft over the base, an independent
 * critic reads the recorded bytes, and the exit's approval carries the
 * drafted floor. What a person or the policy approved is written into the
 * artifact root and the base, and locked against that hash (I3).
 *
 * Every step is recorded before the next reads it, in run state's attempt for
 * the station, so a resume does only what is not recorded. Nothing here takes
 * a status from a model (I2): a draft is a claim until it is approved and
 * locked, and a critic's clean result is one condition among others the
 * runtime checks itself, never sufficient (D-A-DS-07).
 */
import { createHash } from 'node:crypto';
import { mkdir, readFile, rm } from 'node:fs/promises';
import { join } from 'node:path';
import {
  capabilityRefusal,
  CRITIC_CONTRACTS,
  DRAFT_ROLES,
  gradeAgainst,
  gradeOf,
  grantedContext,
  grantedCriticContext,
  MODEL_TIERS,
  retrySuffix,
  seatTask,
  seatTestDrafter,
  STATION_CONTRACTS,
  stationTier,
  tierFor,
  tierRank,
  weakerGrade,
} from '@olympus-ai/core';
import type {
  BaseRefusalPart,
  ContextGrant,
  DraftAttempt,
  DraftCondition,
  DraftFloor,
  DraftRef,
  DraftSendBack,
  DraftSeat,
  DraftState,
  DraftStation,
  Driver,
  ModelIdentity,
  RetryPart,
  RunState,
  StationRefusal,
  VaultRef,
} from '@olympus-ai/core';
import type { CheckSpec } from '@olympus-ai/integrity';
import { draftDigest } from '@olympus-ai/vault';
import type { BaseRunRecord, CriticRecord, DecisionCause, DraftFile, DraftLockRecord, DraftRecord, Vault } from '@olympus-ai/vault';
import { baseMismatches, baseRefusal } from './base.js';
import { recordDecision } from './decisions.js';
import {
  attemptsOf,
  commit,
  joinParts,
  pinnedRole,
  readRecord,
  readText,
  recordTamper,
  runOnBase,
  runTask,
  scopeFor,
  spendRetry,
  specArtifacts,
  startAttempt,
  tamperedPaths,
  type LineArtifacts,
  type LineContext,
} from './line.js';
import { denialRefusal, parseSecurityPart, type SecurityPart } from './security.js';
import { DRAFT_DIR, parseQuestions, parseRunRequirements, type RunRequirements } from './spec-parts.js';
import { mergedCheckProblems, parseManifest } from './validate.js';
import { suiteBindingProblems, taskResultProblems } from './verification.js';
import { basePath, copyOnly, copyTree, draftBaseRunPath, draftPaths, existsIn, ownDiff, scratchPath, treeDigest, writeFiles } from './workspace.js';

/** The drivers that seat each drafter and each critic (R19). A run that drafts is refused without them. */
export interface DraftingDrivers {
  readonly specDrafter: Driver;
  readonly specCritic: Driver;
  readonly testDrafter: Driver;
  readonly testCritic: Driver;
}

/** What a run was admitted to draft, with the intent and rubrics it was admitted with (R19). */
export interface DraftingContext {
  readonly drafted: Readonly<Record<DraftStation, boolean>>;
  readonly intent: string | null;
  readonly rubrics: Readonly<Partial<Record<DraftStation, { readonly text: string; readonly sha256: string }>>>;
}

export const NOT_DRAFTING: DraftingContext = Object.freeze({ drafted: Object.freeze({ spec: false, 'test-design': false }), intent: null, rubrics: Object.freeze({}) });

/** Where a drafted spec's files are written, in the drafter's tree and in the artifact root alike. */
export function specDraftPaths(runId: string): { readonly spec: string; readonly security: string; readonly runRequirements: string; readonly questions: string } {
  const dir = `${DRAFT_DIR}/${runId}`;
  return { spec: `${dir}/spec.md`, security: `${dir}/security.json`, runRequirements: `${dir}/run-requirements.json`, questions: `${dir}/questions.json` };
}

/** Where a drafted acceptance manifest is written. */
export function manifestDraftPath(runId: string): string {
  return `${DRAFT_DIR}/${runId}/acceptance.json`;
}

export function seatDriver(drivers: DraftingDrivers, station: DraftStation, seat: DraftSeat): Driver {
  if (station === 'spec') return seat === 'drafter' ? drivers.specDrafter : drivers.specCritic;
  return seat === 'drafter' ? drivers.testDrafter : drivers.testCritic;
}

export function isDrafted(ctx: Pick<LineContext, 'drafting'>, station: DraftStation): boolean {
  return ctx.drafting.drafted[station];
}

function draftsOf(state: RunState, station: DraftStation): DraftState {
  return state.drafts[station] ?? { attempts: [], locked: null, parked: false };
}

/** The floor a drafted exit carries: the station's last draft and the conditions found failed for it when it reached the gate. */
export function draftFloorOf(state: RunState, station: DraftStation): DraftFloor {
  const last = draftsOf(state, station).attempts.at(-1);
  if (last?.sha256 == null || last.failed === null) throw new Error(`line: ${station} reached its exit with no draft at the gate`);
  return { draft: { sha256: last.sha256, attempt: last.attempt }, failed: last.failed };
}

/** The draft the locked acceptance tests came from, or null for tests the run was admitted with: which base record `verify` reads. */
export function testDraftOf(ctx: Pick<LineContext, 'drafting' | 'state'>): string | null {
  if (!isDrafted(ctx, 'test-design')) return null;
  return draftsOf(ctx.state, 'test-design').attempts.at(-1)?.sha256 ?? null;
}

function maxAttempts(station: DraftStation): number {
  return STATION_CONTRACTS[station].maxIterations;
}

function emptyAttempt(attempt: number): DraftAttempt {
  return { attempt, draft: null, sha256: null, critic: null, baseRun: null, failed: null, sentBack: null };
}

/** Replaces the station's last attempt with `change` applied, in one commit. */
async function updateAttempt(ctx: LineContext, station: DraftStation, change: Partial<DraftAttempt>, extra: { phase?: RunState['phase'] } = {}): Promise<void> {
  const drafts = draftsOf(ctx.state, station);
  const last = drafts.attempts.at(-1);
  if (last === undefined) throw new Error(`line: ${station} has no attempt to update`);
  await commit(ctx, { ...extra, drafts: { [station]: { ...drafts, attempts: [...drafts.attempts.slice(0, -1), { ...last, ...change }] } } });
}

/**
 * The station's drafting, from what run state records: a new attempt where
 * the last went back, the drafter where it has not drafted, then the base run
 * of a test draft, then the critic, then the gate. Each step is committed
 * before the next, and an attempt sent back is never resumed. Returns a
 * refusal, or undefined once the draft is at the gate and the station is
 * exiting.
 */
export async function draftStation(ctx: LineContext, station: DraftStation): Promise<StationRefusal | undefined> {
  for (;;) {
    const drafts = draftsOf(ctx.state, station);
    const current = drafts.attempts.at(-1);
    if (current?.sentBack !== null) {
      const attempt = (current?.attempt ?? 0) + 1;
      // An attempt goes back only while another is allowed; the last goes to a person, whose rejection parks the run.
      if (attempt > maxAttempts(station)) throw new Error(`line: ${station} ran past its ${String(maxAttempts(station))} attempts`);
      await commit(ctx, { drafts: { [station]: { ...drafts, attempts: [...drafts.attempts, emptyAttempt(attempt)] } } });
      continue;
    }
    if (current.draft === null) {
      const refusal = await draft(ctx, station, current, drafts.attempts.at(-2) ?? null);
      if (refusal !== undefined) return refusal;
      continue;
    }
    const record = await readRecord<DraftRecord>(ctx, current.draft);
    const last = current.attempt >= maxAttempts(station);
    if (record.problems.length > 0) {
      if (!last) {
        await sendBack(ctx, station, current, { by: 'runtime', problems: record.problems });
        continue;
      }
      return toGate(ctx, station, current, record);
    }
    if (station === 'test-design') {
      if (current.baseRun === null) {
        const refusal = await baseRunDraft(ctx, record);
        if (refusal !== undefined) return refusal;
        continue;
      }
      const judged = await judgeBase(ctx, current.baseRun, record);
      if (judged.refusal !== undefined) return judged.refusal;
      if (judged.parts.length > 0 && !last) {
        await sendBack(ctx, station, current, { by: 'base-run' }, { by: 'base-run', baseRun: current.baseRun, checks: judged.parts });
        continue;
      }
    }
    if (current.critic === null) {
      const refusal = await critique(ctx, station, current, record);
      if (refusal !== undefined) return refusal;
      continue;
    }
    if (current.critic !== 'not-seated') {
      const critic = await readRecord<CriticRecord>(ctx, current.critic);
      // A critic of the drafter's family is advisory: its findings are shown to the person and send nothing back (D-A-DS-06).
      if (critic.binding && critic.findings.length > 0 && !last) {
        await sendBack(ctx, station, current, { by: 'critic' }, { by: 'critic', critic: current.critic, findings: critic.findings.length });
        continue;
      }
    }
    return toGate(ctx, station, current, record);
  }
}

/** Records why a draft went back, then ends its attempt: the decision before the state, as a park is (D-P14-07). */
async function sendBack(
  ctx: LineContext, station: DraftStation, current: DraftAttempt, sentBack: DraftSendBack,
  decided?: Extract<DecisionCause, { cause: 'draft-sent-back' }>['sentBack'],
): Promise<void> {
  if (current.sha256 === null) throw new Error(`line: ${station} cannot send back an attempt that drafted nothing`);
  const draft: DraftRef = { sha256: current.sha256, attempt: current.attempt };
  const why = decided ?? (sentBack.by === 'runtime' ? { by: 'runtime' as const, problems: sentBack.problems } : undefined);
  if (why === undefined) throw new Error(`line: a ${sentBack.by} send-back carries what it is decided from`);
  await recordDecision({ vault: ctx.components.vault, runId: ctx.run.id, taskId: null, station }, { cause: 'draft-sent-back', decidedBy: 'line', draft, sentBack: why });
  await updateAttempt(ctx, station, { sentBack });
}

/** The draft at the gate: the runtime's conditions evaluated and recorded with the attempt, and the station exiting. */
async function toGate(ctx: LineContext, station: DraftStation, current: DraftAttempt, record: DraftRecord): Promise<undefined> {
  const failed = await conditionsFailed(ctx, station, current, record);
  await updateAttempt(ctx, station, { failed }, { phase: 'exiting' });
  return undefined;
}

/**
 * Each condition of the drafted floor that does not hold for this draft, by
 * name (D-A-DS-04). A condition the runtime cannot evaluate has failed (I5):
 * a questions part that does not parse has open questions as far as the
 * floor knows, and a critic that was not seated returned no clean result.
 */
async function conditionsFailed(ctx: LineContext, station: DraftStation, current: DraftAttempt, record: DraftRecord): Promise<DraftCondition[]> {
  const failed: DraftCondition[] = [];
  if (record.problems.length > 0) failed.push('shape');
  if (station === 'spec') {
    const questions = fileOf(record, specDraftPaths(ctx.run.id).questions);
    const parsed = questions === undefined ? undefined : parseQuestions(jsonOf(questions), 'questions');
    if (parsed === undefined || !parsed.ok || parsed.part.openQuestions.length > 0) failed.push('no-open-question');
  }
  let clean = false;
  if (current.critic !== null && current.critic !== 'not-seated') {
    const critic = await readRecord<CriticRecord>(ctx, current.critic);
    clean = critic.binding && critic.findings.length === 0;
  }
  if (!clean) failed.push('independent-critic-clean');
  if (station === 'test-design') {
    const passed = current.baseRun !== null && (await judgeBase(ctx, current.baseRun, record)).parts.length === 0;
    if (!passed) failed.push('base-run-passed');
    if (gradeAgainst(record.model, builderModels(ctx)) !== 'different-family') failed.push('drafter-independent-of-builder');
  }
  return failed;
}

/**
 * Every model the build driver can resolve for this run: each build task's
 * station tier, and each tier its escalation grant can raise it to (R14). A
 * test drafter of any of their families is not independent of the builder.
 */
export function builderModels(ctx: Pick<LineContext, 'graph' | 'policy' | 'components'>): ModelIdentity[] {
  const models = new Map<string, ModelIdentity>();
  for (const task of ctx.graph.tasks) {
    if (task.station !== 'build') continue;
    const scope = scopeFor(ctx as LineContext, task);
    const from = tierRank(stationTier(scope, 'build'));
    const to = scope.escalation === 'none' ? from : Math.max(from, tierRank(scope.escalation.ceiling));
    for (let rank = from; rank <= to; rank++) {
      const tier = MODEL_TIERS[rank];
      if (tier === undefined) continue;
      const model = ctx.components.driver.resolveModel(tier);
      models.set(JSON.stringify([model.provider, model.family, model.model, model.version]), model);
    }
  }
  return [...models.values()];
}

function fileOf(record: DraftRecord, path: string): DraftFile | undefined {
  return record.files.find((f) => f.path === path);
}

function bytesOf(file: DraftFile): Uint8Array {
  return Buffer.from(file.base64, 'base64');
}

function jsonOf(file: DraftFile): unknown {
  try {
    const parsed: unknown = JSON.parse(new TextDecoder('utf-8', { fatal: true }).decode(bytesOf(file)));
    return parsed;
  } catch {
    return undefined;
  }
}

function sha256(bytes: Uint8Array): string {
  return createHash('sha256').update(bytes).digest('hex');
}

/** What the previous attempt's ending tells the next drafter, typed, and nothing else (D-A-DS-02). */
async function retryParts(ctx: LineContext, station: DraftStation, previous: DraftAttempt | null): Promise<RetryPart[]> {
  const back = previous?.sentBack ?? null;
  if (previous === null || back === null) return [];
  switch (back.by) {
    case 'runtime':
      return [{ kind: 'runtime-problems', problems: back.problems }];
    case 'critic': {
      if (previous.critic === null || previous.critic === 'not-seated') return [];
      const critic = await readRecord<CriticRecord>(ctx, previous.critic);
      return [{ kind: 'critic-findings', findings: critic.findings }];
    }
    case 'base-run': {
      if (previous.baseRun === null || previous.draft === null) return [];
      const record = await readRecord<DraftRecord>(ctx, previous.draft);
      return [{ kind: 'base-refusal', checks: (await judgeBase(ctx, previous.baseRun, record)).parts }];
    }
    case 'principal':
      if (station === 'spec') return back.notes === null ? [] : [{ kind: 'notes', notes: back.notes }];
      return back.reason === null ? [] : [{ kind: 'rejection-reason', reason: back.reason }];
  }
}

/**
 * Runs the drafter for the station's current attempt and records what it
 * drafted. The spec drafter works over a fresh copy of the base; the test
 * drafter over a tree holding the locked spec and nothing else, so it is
 * black-box by what it can reach, not by its instructions (I3).
 */
async function draft(ctx: LineContext, station: DraftStation, current: DraftAttempt, previous: DraftAttempt | null): Promise<StationRefusal | undefined> {
  const drivers = draftingDrivers(ctx);
  const driver = seatDriver(drivers, station, 'drafter');
  const seat = seatTask(ctx.state, station, 'drafter', ctx.run.baseCommit);
  const capability = capabilityRefusal(STATION_CONTRACTS[station], driver.capabilities());
  if (capability !== undefined) return capability;
  if ((await ctx.components.vault.readLocks(ctx.run.id)) !== null) {
    const tampered = await tamperedPaths(ctx);
    if (tampered.length > 0) return recordTamper(ctx, station, null, tampered, { phase: 'before-draft' }, null);
  }
  // At L3 a test drafter of the builder's family is refused, as a same-family reviewer is (R9, R19).
  if (station === 'test-design') {
    const model = driver.resolveModel(tierFor(scopeFor(ctx, seat), station, attemptsOf(ctx.state, seat)));
    const seated = seatTestDrafter(model, builderModels(ctx), ctx.run.requestedLevel);
    if (!seated.ok) return seated;
  }

  let context: string;
  if (station === 'spec') {
    context = joinParts(grantedContext(STATION_CONTRACTS.spec, { intent: ctx.drafting.intent ?? undefined } as Partial<Record<ContextGrant, string>>));
  } else {
    const spec = await readText(ctx, specArtifacts(ctx));
    if (spec.tampered.length > 0) return recordTamper(ctx, station, null, spec.tampered, { phase: 'context' }, null);
    context = joinParts(grantedContext(STATION_CONTRACTS['test-design'], { 'locked-spec': spec.text }));
  }
  const suffix = retrySuffix(station, current.attempt, await retryParts(ctx, station, previous));

  const started = await startAttempt(ctx, seat);
  if (started !== undefined) return started;
  const { workspaces } = ctx.components;
  const { start, work } = draftPaths(workspaces, ctx.run.id, station, 'drafter', current.attempt);
  const base = basePath(workspaces, ctx.run.id);
  if (station === 'spec') await copyTree(base, start);
  else await copyOnly(base, start, specArtifacts(ctx).map((a) => a.path));
  await copyTree(start, work);
  // The run's drafts directory, so where a drafter writes its draft is in the tree it is handed.
  await mkdir(join(work, DRAFT_DIR, ctx.run.id), { recursive: true });

  const role = pinnedRole(ctx, DRAFT_ROLES[station].drafter);
  const ran = await runTask(ctx, seat, driver, role, context, work, { suffix });
  if (!ran.ok) return spendRetry(ctx, seat);
  const problems = taskResultProblems(ran.result);
  if (problems.length > 0) {
    throw new Error(`line: the drafter's result at ${station} does not match the TaskResult contract, so it was not recorded: ${problems.join('; ')}`);
  }
  const result = await ctx.components.vault.recordTaskResult(ctx.run.id, ran.result);
  await commit(ctx, { tasks: { [seat.id]: 'verifying' }, results: { [seat.id]: result } });

  const read = station === 'spec' ? await readSpecDraft(ctx, start, work) : await readTestDraft(ctx, start, work);
  const record: DraftRecord = {
    runId: ctx.run.id,
    station,
    attempt: current.attempt,
    sha256: draftDigest(read.files),
    files: read.files,
    discarded: read.discarded,
    problems: read.problems,
    // The model the runtime resolved for the call, never the driver's account of itself (I2).
    model: ran.model,
    role: { id: role.role, sha256: role.sha256 },
    collectedBy: 'runtime',
  };
  const ref = await ctx.components.vault.recordDraft(record);
  await updateAttempt(ctx, station, { draft: ref, sha256: record.sha256 });
  return undefined;
}

interface ReadDraft {
  readonly files: DraftFile[];
  readonly discarded: string[];
  readonly problems: Array<{ path: string; code: string }>;
}

async function fileAt(root: string, path: string): Promise<DraftFile | undefined> {
  try {
    const bytes = await readFile(join(root, ...path.split('/')));
    return { path, sha256: sha256(bytes), base64: Buffer.from(bytes).toString('base64') };
  } catch {
    return undefined;
  }
}

/** What the drafter wrote outside the paths it drafts, which its workspace takes with it. */
async function discardedOf(start: string, work: string, kept: (path: string) => boolean): Promise<string[]> {
  return (await ownDiff(start, work)).map((entry) => entry.path).filter((path) => !kept(path)).sort();
}

/** A spec draft's four files, each read and parsed from the bytes read; anything wrong is a problem, by path and code. */
async function readSpecDraft(ctx: LineContext, start: string, work: string): Promise<ReadDraft> {
  const paths = specDraftPaths(ctx.run.id);
  const all = [paths.spec, paths.security, paths.runRequirements, paths.questions];
  const files: DraftFile[] = [];
  const problems: Array<{ path: string; code: string }> = [];
  for (const path of all) {
    const file = await fileAt(work, path);
    if (file === undefined) problems.push({ path, code: 'missing' });
    else files.push(file);
  }
  const spec = files.find((f) => f.path === paths.spec);
  if (spec !== undefined) {
    let text = '';
    try {
      text = new TextDecoder('utf-8', { fatal: true }).decode(bytesOf(spec));
    } catch {
      problems.push({ path: paths.spec, code: 'not-utf8' });
    }
    if (text.trim() === '' && !problems.some((p) => p.path === paths.spec)) problems.push({ path: paths.spec, code: 'empty' });
  }
  const parse = (path: string, parser: (value: unknown) => { ok: boolean; problems?: ReadonlyArray<{ path: string; code: string }> }): void => {
    const file = files.find((f) => f.path === path);
    if (file === undefined) return;
    const json = jsonOf(file);
    if (json === undefined) {
      problems.push({ path, code: 'not-json' });
      return;
    }
    const parsed = parser(json);
    if (!parsed.ok) for (const p of parsed.problems ?? []) problems.push({ path: `${path}#${p.path}`, code: p.code });
  };
  parse(paths.security, (v) => parseSecurityPart(v));
  parse(paths.runRequirements, (v) => parseRunRequirements(v, 'runRequirements'));
  parse(paths.questions, (v) => parseQuestions(v, 'questions'));
  return { files, discarded: await discardedOf(start, work, (p) => all.includes(p)), problems };
}

/** A path is under `dir` when it is `dir/…`. */
function under(dir: string, path: string): boolean {
  return path.startsWith(`${dir}/`);
}

/**
 * A test draft: every file under the spec's test directory and the
 * acceptance manifest, read from the bytes read, and every refusal R15 and
 * R17 make of a manifest at admission made of it here (R19): the drafted
 * manifest held alone to its own shape, to acceptance and behavioral checks,
 * to naming every drafted test, beside the verification manifest held to a
 * reproduction and distinct ids, to a check denying each authorization rule,
 * and to a framework that runs each acceptance check's tests exactly, over
 * the base with the draft in it. Problems are paths and codes, never base
 * output, which carries the repository.
 */
async function readTestDraft(ctx: LineContext, start: string, work: string): Promise<ReadDraft> {
  const requirements = ctx.requirements;
  if (requirements === null) throw new Error('line: drafted tests reached test-design with no run-requirements part locked; admission refuses that');
  const manifestPath = manifestDraftPath(ctx.run.id);
  const testDir = requirements.testDirectory;
  const specPaths = new Set(specArtifacts(ctx).map((a) => a.path));
  const drafted = (path: string): boolean => path === manifestPath || (under(testDir, path) && !specPaths.has(path));
  const changed = (await ownDiff(start, work)).filter((e) => e.sha256 !== null).map((e) => e.path);
  const files: DraftFile[] = [];
  for (const path of changed.filter(drafted).sort()) {
    const file = await fileAt(work, path);
    if (file !== undefined) files.push(file);
  }
  const problems: Array<{ path: string; code: string }> = [];
  const tests = files.filter((f) => f.path !== manifestPath).map((f) => f.path);
  if (tests.length === 0) problems.push({ path: testDir, code: 'missing' });
  const base = basePath(ctx.components.workspaces, ctx.run.id);
  for (const test of tests) if (await existsIn(base, test)) problems.push({ path: test, code: 'exists' });
  const manifest = files.find((f) => f.path === manifestPath);
  const checks = manifest === undefined ? undefined : draftedChecks(manifest, problems);
  if (manifest === undefined) problems.push({ path: manifestPath, code: 'missing' });
  if (checks !== undefined) {
    const named = new Set<string>();
    checks.forEach((check, i) => {
      const at = `${manifestPath}#checks[${String(i)}]`;
      if (check.kind !== 'acceptance' && check.kind !== 'behavioral') {
        problems.push({ path: at, code: 'not-draftable' });
        return;
      }
      const runs = check.kind === 'acceptance' ? [...check.tests] : check.command.filter((arg) => tests.includes(arg));
      for (const test of runs) {
        if (!tests.includes(test)) problems.push({ path: `${at}.tests`, code: 'unreferenced' });
        named.add(test);
      }
    });
    for (const test of tests) if (!named.has(test)) problems.push({ path: test, code: 'unreferenced' });
    for (const p of mergedCheckProblems(ctx.checks, checks)) problems.push({ path: `${manifestPath}#${p.path}`, code: p.code });
    const undenied = denialRefusal(ctx.securityPart, [...ctx.checks, ...checks]);
    if (undenied?.reason === 'denial-check-missing') for (const rule of undenied.rules) problems.push({ path: `${manifestPath}#deniesRule:${rule}`, code: 'denial-check-missing' });
    if (problems.length === 0) {
      // Each acceptance check's tests bound to the base's framework, over the base with the draft written in (D-R15-04).
      const tree = await draftTree(ctx, files);
      for (const p of await suiteBindingProblems(checks, tree, tests)) problems.push({ path: `${manifestPath}#${p.path}`, code: p.code });
    }
  }
  return { files, discarded: await discardedOf(start, work, (p) => drafted(p)), problems };
}

/** The drafted manifest's checks, held alone to a manifest's shape; null where it is not one, its problems added. */
function draftedChecks(manifest: DraftFile, problems: Array<{ path: string; code: string }>): CheckSpec[] | undefined {
  const json = jsonOf(manifest);
  if (json === undefined) {
    problems.push({ path: manifest.path, code: 'not-json' });
    return undefined;
  }
  const parsed = parseManifest(json, { alone: false });
  if (!parsed.ok) {
    for (const p of parsed.problems) problems.push({ path: `${manifest.path}#${p.path}`, code: p.code });
    return undefined;
  }
  return parsed.checks;
}

/** The base with a test draft's files written in: what its checks run over and what its tests are bound against (R19). */
async function draftTree(ctx: LineContext, files: readonly DraftFile[]): Promise<string> {
  const { workspaces } = ctx.components;
  const tree = draftBaseRunPath(workspaces, ctx.run.id, draftDigest(files));
  await copyTree(basePath(workspaces, ctx.run.id), tree);
  await writeFiles(tree, files.map((f) => ({ path: f.path, bytes: bytesOf(f) })));
  return tree;
}

/** The checks a test draft is judged with: the verification manifest's and its own. */
function checksWithDraft(ctx: LineContext, record: DraftRecord): CheckSpec[] {
  const manifest = fileOf(record, manifestDraftPath(ctx.run.id));
  const parsed = manifest === undefined ? undefined : parseManifest(jsonOf(manifest), { alone: false });
  if (parsed?.ok !== true) throw new Error(`line: test draft ${record.sha256} reached its base run without a manifest that parses`);
  return [...ctx.checks, ...parsed.checks];
}

/**
 * Runs the draft's checks, and the verification manifest's, over the base
 * with the draft in it, once per draft hash, and records the result under
 * that hash (R19, D-A-BF-01). A record already written for the hash is the
 * one used: a resume never runs them twice.
 */
async function baseRunDraft(ctx: LineContext, record: DraftRecord): Promise<StationRefusal | undefined> {
  let ref = await ctx.components.vault.readBaseRun(ctx.run.id, record.sha256);
  if (ref === null) {
    const tree = await draftTree(ctx, record.files);
    const ran = await runOnBase(ctx, { tree, checks: checksWithDraft(ctx, record), draft: record.sha256 });
    if (!('hash' in ran)) return ran;
    ref = ran;
  }
  await updateAttempt(ctx, 'test-design', { baseRun: ref });
  return undefined;
}

/**
 * The base record of a test draft, judged: each drafted check that did not do
 * what it declared, as the next attempt may learn it (D-A-DS-01), and a
 * refusal of the run where a check the run was admitted with did not, which
 * no draft caused and no redraft cures.
 */
async function judgeBase(ctx: LineContext, ref: VaultRef, record: DraftRecord): Promise<{ parts: BaseRefusalPart[]; refusal?: StationRefusal }> {
  const base = await readRecord<BaseRunRecord>(ctx, ref);
  const checks = checksWithDraft(ctx, record);
  const admitted = new Set(ctx.checks.map((c) => c.id));
  const mismatched = baseMismatches(checks, base);
  const own = mismatched.filter((m) => admitted.has(m.checkId));
  if (own.length > 0) {
    const refusal = baseRefusal(ctx.checks, base, ref);
    if (refusal !== undefined) return { parts: [], refusal };
  }
  const parts = mismatched.filter((m) => !admitted.has(m.checkId)).map((m): BaseRefusalPart =>
    m.cause === 'contrary' && m.exitCode !== null
      ? { checkId: m.checkId, declared: m.declared, exitCode: m.exitCode }
      : { checkId: m.checkId, declared: m.declared, cause: m.cause === 'suite-count' ? 'suite-short' : 'did-not-start' });
  return { parts };
}

/** The draft's files as the critic reads them: each path, then its text. */
function renderDraft(record: DraftRecord): string {
  return record.files.map((f) => `--- ${f.path}\n${new TextDecoder().decode(bytesOf(f))}`).join('\n');
}

/**
 * A critic's result is its final message, `{"findings": [string, …]}`, with no
 * severity. Anything else is one finding: a result the runtime cannot read
 * is never a clean one (I5, D-A-DS-07). A single fenced block is unwrapped.
 */
export function readCriticResult(narrative: string): { readable: boolean; findings: string[] } {
  const unreadable = { readable: false, findings: ['the critic returned no result the runtime could read as a list of findings'] };
  const text = narrative.trim().replace(/^```(?:json)?\s*\n([\s\S]*)\n```$/, '$1').trim();
  let parsed: unknown;
  try {
    parsed = JSON.parse(text);
  } catch {
    return unreadable;
  }
  if (typeof parsed !== 'object' || parsed === null || Array.isArray(parsed)) return unreadable;
  const keys = Object.keys(parsed);
  const { findings } = parsed as { findings?: unknown };
  if (keys.length !== 1 || !Array.isArray(findings) || !findings.every((f) => typeof f === 'string' && f.trim() !== '')) return unreadable;
  return { readable: true, findings: findings as string[] };
}

/**
 * Seats the critic over the recorded bytes, read-only, granted what the
 * drafter was, the draft, and the rubric (R19). Graded against the drafter
 * from the models the runtime resolved, and again from the model its result
 * names, keeping the weaker (I6). Where it would be the drafter's own model,
 * no critic is seated: the condition fails and the draft goes to a person.
 */
async function critique(ctx: LineContext, station: DraftStation, current: DraftAttempt, record: DraftRecord): Promise<StationRefusal | undefined> {
  const driver = seatDriver(draftingDrivers(ctx), station, 'critic');
  const seat = seatTask(ctx.state, station, 'critic', ctx.run.baseCommit);
  const contract = CRITIC_CONTRACTS[station];
  const capability = capabilityRefusal({ ...STATION_CONTRACTS[station], requires: contract.requires }, driver.capabilities());
  if (capability !== undefined) return capability;
  const planned = driver.resolveModel(tierFor(scopeFor(ctx, seat), station, attemptsOf(ctx.state, seat)));
  if (gradeOf(record.model, planned) === 'same-model') {
    await updateAttempt(ctx, station, { critic: 'not-seated' });
    return undefined;
  }
  const rubric = ctx.drafting.rubrics[station];
  if (rubric === undefined) throw new Error(`line: no rubric was admitted for the ${station} critic; admission refuses that`);

  const offered: Partial<Record<ContextGrant, string>> = { draft: renderDraft(record), rubric: rubric.text };
  if (station === 'spec') {
    if (ctx.drafting.intent !== null) offered.intent = ctx.drafting.intent;
  } else {
    const spec = await readText(ctx, specArtifacts(ctx));
    if (spec.tampered.length > 0) return recordTamper(ctx, station, null, spec.tampered, { phase: 'context' }, null);
    offered['locked-spec'] = spec.text;
  }
  const context = joinParts(grantedCriticContext(contract, offered));

  const started = await startAttempt(ctx, seat);
  if (started !== undefined) return started;
  const { workspaces } = ctx.components;
  const { work } = draftPaths(workspaces, ctx.run.id, station, 'critic', current.attempt);
  const base = basePath(workspaces, ctx.run.id);
  if (station === 'spec') await copyTree(base, work);
  else await copyOnly(base, work, specArtifacts(ctx).map((a) => a.path));
  await writeFiles(work, record.files.map((f) => ({ path: f.path, bytes: bytesOf(f) })));

  const role = pinnedRole(ctx, DRAFT_ROLES[station].critic);
  // Which attempt's draft it reads, and nothing else of the drafter's history.
  const ran = await runTask(ctx, seat, driver, role, context, work, { mode: 'ro', suffix: retrySuffix(station, current.attempt, []) });
  if (!ran.ok) return spendRetry(ctx, seat);
  const problems = taskResultProblems(ran.result);
  if (problems.length > 0) {
    throw new Error(`line: the critic's result at ${station} does not match the TaskResult contract, so it was not recorded: ${problems.join('; ')}`);
  }
  const result = await ctx.components.vault.recordTaskResult(ctx.run.id, ran.result);
  await commit(ctx, { tasks: { [seat.id]: 'passed' }, results: { [seat.id]: result } });
  const read = readCriticResult(ran.result.claim.narrative);
  // The weaker of the planned seat and the one the result names: what the critic reports never widens it (I2, I6).
  const grade = weakerGrade(gradeOf(record.model, planned), gradeOf(record.model, ran.result.model));
  const critic: CriticRecord = {
    runId: ctx.run.id,
    station,
    attempt: current.attempt,
    draft: record.sha256,
    drafter: record.model,
    critic: ran.model,
    grade,
    binding: grade === 'different-family',
    readable: read.readable,
    findings: read.findings,
    rubric: rubric.sha256,
    role: { id: role.role, sha256: role.sha256 },
    collectedBy: 'runtime',
  };
  const ref = await ctx.components.vault.recordCriticResult(critic);
  await updateAttempt(ctx, station, { critic: ref });
  return undefined;
}

function draftingDrivers(ctx: LineContext): DraftingDrivers {
  const drivers = ctx.components.drafting ?? null;
  if (drivers === null) throw new Error('line: the run drafts and its component graph names no drafting drivers; admission refuses that');
  return drivers;
}

/**
 * The artifacts a locked draft makes of the line's: a spec's file and its
 * three parts, or the tests and their manifest (R19). Pure, from the lock's
 * paths, so a drive and a resume derive the same set.
 */
export function lockedArtifacts(artifacts: LineArtifacts, runId: string, station: DraftStation, files: ReadonlyArray<{ readonly path: string; readonly sha256: string }>): LineArtifacts {
  const at = (path: string): { path: string; sha256: string } | null => files.find((f) => f.path === path) ?? null;
  if (station === 'spec') {
    const paths = specDraftPaths(runId);
    const spec = at(paths.spec);
    return {
      ...artifacts,
      spec: spec === null ? [] : [spec],
      specParts: { security: at(paths.security), runRequirements: at(paths.runRequirements), questions: at(paths.questions) },
    };
  }
  const manifest = manifestDraftPath(runId);
  return { ...artifacts, acceptanceTests: files.filter((f) => f.path !== manifest), acceptanceManifest: at(manifest) };
}

/**
 * The approved draft written into the artifact root and the base, and
 * locked against its hash (R19). The lock record comes first, with the base
 * digest the draft makes, and is committed before anything is written; then
 * the files, then the Vault's lock of each path not already locked. Every
 * step can be taken again by a resume, and the lock is held to the approved
 * hash: bytes changed in the artifact root after the draft was read are a
 * tamper, never what is locked (I3).
 */
export async function lockApproved(ctx: LineContext, station: DraftStation, approval: DraftLockRecord['approval']): Promise<StationRefusal | undefined> {
  const { vault, workspaces } = ctx.components;
  const drafts = draftsOf(ctx.state, station);
  const current = drafts.attempts.at(-1);
  if (current?.draft == null || current.sha256 === null) throw new Error(`line: ${station} approved no draft`);
  const record = await readRecord<DraftRecord>(ctx, current.draft);
  if (record.problems.length > 0 || record.files.length === 0) throw new Error(`line: ${station}'s draft ${record.sha256} carries problems and cannot be locked; approval refuses it`);
  const base = basePath(workspaces, ctx.run.id);
  let lock: DraftLockRecord;
  if (drafts.locked === null) {
    // The digest the base will have, computed on a copy, so the base itself is changed only once the lock is recorded.
    const scratch = scratchPath(workspaces, ctx.run.id, `lock.${station}`);
    await copyTree(base, scratch);
    await writeFiles(scratch, record.files.map((f) => ({ path: f.path, bytes: bytesOf(f) })));
    const digest = await treeDigest(scratch);
    await rm(scratch, { recursive: true, force: true });
    lock = {
      runId: ctx.run.id,
      station,
      draft: { sha256: record.sha256, attempt: record.attempt },
      files: record.files.map(({ path, sha256: hash }) => ({ path, sha256: hash })),
      approval,
      baseTreeSha256: digest,
      collectedBy: 'runtime',
    };
    const ref = await vault.recordDraftLock(lock);
    await commit(ctx, { drafts: { [station]: { ...draftsOf(ctx.state, station), locked: ref } } });
  } else {
    lock = await readRecord<DraftLockRecord>(ctx, drafts.locked);
  }

  const held = new Set((await vault.readLocks(ctx.run.id))?.entries.map((e) => e.path) ?? []);
  const unlocked = record.files.filter((f) => !held.has(f.path));
  // A path in the artifact root that holds other bytes than the draft is not overwritten: what is there is not ours to replace.
  const clobbered: Array<{ path: string; expected: string; actual: string }> = [];
  for (const file of unlocked) {
    const there = await fileAt(ctx.run.repo, file.path);
    if (there !== undefined && there.sha256 !== file.sha256) clobbered.push({ path: file.path, expected: file.sha256, actual: there.sha256 });
  }
  if (clobbered.length > 0) return recordTamper(ctx, station, null, clobbered, { phase: 'draft-lock' }, null);
  await writeFiles(ctx.run.repo, unlocked.map((f) => ({ path: f.path, bytes: bytesOf(f) })));
  await writeFiles(base, record.files.map((f) => ({ path: f.path, bytes: bytesOf(f) })));
  if (unlocked.length > 0) await vault.lock(ctx.run.id, unlocked.map((f) => f.path), station);
  const manifest = await vault.readLocks(ctx.run.id);
  const tampered = record.files.flatMap((file) => {
    const entry = manifest?.entries.find((e) => e.path === file.path);
    return entry?.sha256 === file.sha256 ? [] : [{ path: file.path, expected: file.sha256, actual: entry?.sha256 ?? 'missing' }];
  });
  if (tampered.length > 0) return recordTamper(ctx, station, null, tampered, { phase: 'draft-lock' }, null);
  const digest = await treeDigest(base);
  if (digest !== lock.baseTreeSha256) throw new Error(`line: the base is ${digest} after ${station}'s draft was written, not the ${lock.baseTreeSha256} its lock recorded`);

  applyLock(ctx, station, record);
  ctx.baseTreeSha256 = lock.baseTreeSha256;
  return undefined;
}

/** The line's artifacts, checks, and spec parts once `record` is locked at `station`, parsed from the bytes it holds. */
function applyLock(ctx: LineContext, station: DraftStation, record: DraftRecord): void {
  ctx.artifacts = lockedArtifacts(ctx.artifacts, ctx.run.id, station, record.files);
  if (station === 'spec') {
    const paths = specDraftPaths(ctx.run.id);
    const security = fileOf(record, paths.security);
    const requirements = fileOf(record, paths.runRequirements);
    const part = security === undefined ? undefined : parseSecurityPart(jsonOf(security));
    const req = requirements === undefined ? undefined : parseRunRequirements(jsonOf(requirements), 'runRequirements');
    ctx.securityPart = part?.ok === true ? part.part : null;
    ctx.requirements = req?.ok === true ? req.part : null;
    return;
  }
  ctx.checks = checksWithDraft(ctx, record);
}

/** The text of each spec part locked or admitted, parsed: what a drive started from the records holds. */
export function parsedParts(texts: { security: unknown; runRequirements: unknown }): { securityPart: SecurityPart | null; requirements: RunRequirements | null } {
  const part = texts.security === undefined ? undefined : parseSecurityPart(texts.security);
  const req = texts.runRequirements === undefined ? undefined : parseRunRequirements(texts.runRequirements, 'runRequirements');
  return { securityPart: part?.ok === true ? part.part : null, requirements: req?.ok === true ? req.part : null };
}

/**
 * Writes each committed draft lock's files into the base again, so a base a
 * stop left half-written is whole before a resume checks its digest (R19).
 * Only the base: the artifact root holds locked bytes the runtime never
 * rewrites, since a rewrite there would launder a tamper.
 */
export async function settleBase(vault: Vault, base: string, state: RunState): Promise<string | null> {
  let digest: string | null = null;
  for (const station of ['spec', 'test-design'] as const) {
    const lockRef = state.drafts[station]?.locked ?? null;
    if (lockRef === null) continue;
    const lock = JSON.parse(new TextDecoder().decode(await vault.read(lockRef))) as DraftLockRecord;
    const attempt = state.drafts[station]?.attempts.find((a) => a.attempt === lock.draft.attempt);
    if (attempt?.draft == null) throw new Error(`run ${state.runId}: the ${station} lock names an attempt run state does not hold`);
    const record = JSON.parse(new TextDecoder().decode(await vault.read(attempt.draft))) as DraftRecord;
    await writeFiles(base, record.files.map((f) => ({ path: f.path, bytes: bytesOf(f) })));
    digest = lock.baseTreeSha256;
  }
  return digest;
}

 export function createApiServer(options: ApiServerOptions): ApiServer {
   if (typeof options.token !== 'string' || options.token.length < MIN_TOKEN_LENGTH) {
     throw new Error(`api server: the local token must be at least ${String(MIN_TOKEN_LENGTH)} characters`);

   const tracked = (runId: RunId): Tracked => {
     let entry = runs.get(runId);
     if (entry === undefined) {
       entry = { drive: null, cancel: null, lastOutcome: null, streams: new Set() };
       runs.set(runId, entry);
     }
     return entry;

   const publish = (entry: Tracked, event: RunEvent): void => {
     for (const stream of entry.streams) stream(event);
   };
 
   /** Drives a run in the background; the returned promise never rejects. */
   const drive = (runId: RunId, start: (hooks: DriveHooks) => Promise<RunOutcome>): void => {
     const entry = tracked(runId);
     entry.cancel = null;
     const hooks: DriveHooks = {
       cancelRequested: () => entry.cancel,
       onCommit: (state) => { publish(entry, { event: 'state', data: state }); },
     };
     entry.drive = start(hooks)
       .then((outcome): DriveEnd => ({ kind: 'outcome', outcome }), (error: unknown): DriveEnd => ({ kind: 'error', message: describe(error) }))
       .then((end) => (end.kind === 'error' ? halt(runId, end) : end))

         entry.drive = null;
         entry.cancel = null;
         const standing = await runStanding(components.vault, runId).then((s) => s.standing, () => undefined);
         if (standing !== undefined) publish(entry, { event: 'standing', data: standing });
         publish(entry, { event: 'end', data: end });
       });
   };
 
   /**
    * A drive that ended in an error, rather than a refusal the line committed,
    * left the run's state as it was mid-step, which status would read as open
    * and a cancel would relabel. The halt is committed so the stop is the
    * Vault's record, and survives this process (A-P9-02). A run already
    * cancelled keeps that record instead; if the halt cannot be committed, the
    * drive's end says so.
    */
   const halt = async (runId: RunId, end: DriveEnd & { kind: 'error' }): Promise<DriveEnd> => {
     try {
       const state = await components.vault.readRunState(runId);
       if (state.cancelled !== null || state.halted !== null) return end;
       await components.vault.commitRunState({ ...state, halted: { at: new Date().toISOString(), message: end.message } }, state.version);
       return end;
     } catch (error) {
       return { kind: 'error', message: `${end.message}; the halt could not be recorded: ${describe(error)}` };
     }
   };
 
   /** The per-run report, read from the Vault alone (D-I1b-07). */
   const report = async (runId: RunId): Promise<RunReport> => {
     try {
       await components.vault.readRunState(runId);
     } catch (error) {
       throw new HttpError(404, 'not-found', `no run ${runId}: ${describe(error)}`);
     }
     return runReport(components.vault, runId);
   };
 
   const view = async (runId: RunId): Promise<RunView> => {
     // Taken before the read, not after: a drive that ends while the state is read has committed
     // past what was read, and a view saying `driving: false` beside that state would present a
     // stale standing as settled. Read first, a drive running at the start is still reported.
     const entry = runs.get(runId);
     const driving = entry?.drive != null;
     const lastOutcome = entry?.lastOutcome ?? null;
     let read: { state: RunState; standing: RunView['standing'] };

     } catch (error) {
       throw new HttpError(404, 'not-found', `no run ${runId}: ${describe(error)}`);
     }
     return { runId, state: read.state, standing: read.standing, driving, lastOutcome };
   };

     const policy = await loadPolicyFile(options.policyFile);

     const runId = randomUUID() as RunId;
-    const admission = await admitRun({ runId, ...body, policy: policy.policy, roles: options.rolesDirectory, components });
+    const { intent, ...request } = body;

+      rubrics: options.rubricsDirectory ?? null,
+      policy: policy.policy,
+      roles: options.rolesDirectory,
+      components,
+    });
     if (!admission.ok) throw new HttpError(refusalStatus(admission), 'refused', `run refused: ${admission.reason}`, admission);
     const { admitted } = admission;
     drive(runId, (hooks) => admitted.drive(hooks));
     return { runId, worstCase: admitted.worstCase, state: admitted.state };
   };
 
   const approve = async (runId: RunId, body: ApproveBody): Promise<RunView> => {
     const entry = tracked(runId);
     if (entry.drive !== null) throw new HttpError(409, 'conflict', `run ${runId} is being driven; it is not waiting for an approval`);
     await view(runId);
-    const outcome = await approveStation({ runId, key: body.key, approvedBy: principal, vault: components.vault });
+    const outcome = await approveStation({ runId, key: body.key, draft: body.draft ?? null, approvedBy: principal, vault: components.vault });
+    if (!outcome.ok) throw new HttpError(outcome.reason === 'invalid-request' ? 400 : 409, 'refused', outcome.message, outcome);
+    drive(runId, (hooks) => resumeRun({ runId, components }, hooks));
+    return view(runId);
+  };
+
+  const reject = async (runId: RunId, body: RejectBody): Promise<RunView> => {
+    const entry = tracked(runId);
+    if (entry.drive !== null) throw new HttpError(409, 'conflict', `run ${runId} is being driven; it is not waiting for a decision on a draft`);
+    await view(runId);
+    const outcome = await rejectDraft({ runId, key: body.key, draft: body.draft, notes: body.notes ?? null, reason: body.reason ?? null, rejectedBy: principal, vault: components.vault });
     if (!outcome.ok) throw new HttpError(outcome.reason === 'invalid-request' ? 400 : 409, 'refused', outcome.message, outcome);
+    // A draft sent back is drafted again; a parked run is driven only to record where it stopped.
     drive(runId, (hooks) => resumeRun({ runId, components }, hooks));
     return view(runId);
   };
 
   const cancel = async (runId: RunId): Promise<RunView> => {
     await view(runId);
     const entry = tracked(runId);
     const inFlight = entry.drive;
     if (inFlight !== null) {
       entry.cancel = { by: principal, at: new Date().toISOString() };
       await inFlight;
       const after = await view(runId);
       if (after.state.cancelled === null) {
         throw new HttpError(409, 'refused', `run ${runId} finished before the cancellation was taken; there is nothing to cancel`, after.standing);
       }
       return after;
     }
     const outcome = await cancelRun({ runId, by: principal, vault: components.vault });
     if (!outcome.ok) throw new HttpError(outcome.reason === 'finished' ? 409 : 400, 'refused', outcome.message, outcome);
     const now = await view(runId);
     publish(entry, { event: 'state', data: now.state });
     publish(entry, { event: 'standing', data: now.standing });
     return now;
   };
 
   const events = async (runId: RunId, res: ServerResponse): Promise<void> => {
     const now = await view(runId);
     const entry = tracked(runId);
     res.writeHead(200, { 'content-type': 'text/event-stream; charset=utf-8', 'cache-control': 'no-store', connection: 'keep-alive' });
     open.add(res);
     const write = (event: RunEvent): void => {
       res.write(`event: ${event.event}\ndata: ${JSON.stringify(event.data)}\n\n`);
     };
     const finish = (): void => {
       entry.streams.delete(stream);
       open.delete(res);
       res.end();
     };
     const stream = (event: RunEvent): void => {
       write(event);
       if (event.event === 'end') finish();
     };
     write({ event: 'state', data: now.state });
     write({ event: 'standing', data: now.standing });
     if (entry.drive === null) {
       write({ event: 'end', data: entry.lastOutcome });
       open.delete(res);
       res.end();
       return;
     }
     entry.streams.add(stream);
     res.on('close', () => {
       entry.streams.delete(stream);
       open.delete(res);
     });
   };
 
   const route = async (req: IncomingMessage, res: ServerResponse): Promise<void> => {
     const presented = digest(req.headers.authorization ?? '');
     if (!timingSafeEqual(presented, expected)) throw new HttpError(401, 'unauthorized', 'a valid local token is required');
     const url = new URL(req.url ?? '/', 'http://server.invalid');
     const parts = url.pathname.split('/').filter((p) => p !== '');
     const method = req.method ?? 'GET';
     if (parts[0] === 'outcomes' && parts.length === 1) {
       if (method !== 'GET') throw new HttpError(405, 'method-not-allowed', `${method} ${url.pathname}`);
       send(res, 200, await outcomes(components.vault, outcomeWindow(url.searchParams)));
       return;
     }
     if (parts[0] !== 'runs' || parts.length > 3) throw new HttpError(404, 'not-found', `no route ${url.pathname}`);
     if (parts.length === 1) {
       if (method !== 'POST') throw new HttpError(405, 'method-not-allowed', `${method} ${url.pathname}`);
       send(res, 201, await create(createBody(await readJson(req))));
       return;
     }
     const runId = decodeURIComponent(parts[1] ?? '') as RunId;
     const action = parts[2];
     if (action === undefined) {
       if (method !== 'GET') throw new HttpError(405, 'method-not-allowed', `${method} ${url.pathname}`);
       send(res, 200, await view(runId));
       return;
     }
     if (action === 'report') {
       if (method !== 'GET') throw new HttpError(405, 'method-not-allowed', `${method} ${url.pathname}`);
       send(res, 200, await report(runId));
       return;
     }
     if (action === 'events') {
       if (method !== 'GET') throw new HttpError(405, 'method-not-allowed', `${method} ${url.pathname}`);
       await events(runId, res);
       return;
     }
-    if (action === 'approve' || action === 'cancel') {
+    if (action === 'approve' || action === 'cancel' || action === 'reject') {
       if (method !== 'POST') throw new HttpError(405, 'method-not-allowed', `${method} ${url.pathname}`);
-      send(res, 200, action === 'approve' ? await approve(runId, approveBody(await readJson(req))) : await cancel(runId));
+      if (action === 'approve') send(res, 200, await approve(runId, approveBody(await readJson(req))));
+      else if (action === 'reject') send(res, 200, await reject(runId, rejectBody(await readJson(req))));
+      else send(res, 200, await cancel(runId));
       return;
     }
     throw new HttpError(404, 'not-found', `no route ${url.pathname}`);
   };
 
   const server: Server = createServer((req, res) => {
     route(req, res).catch((error: unknown) => {
       const http = error instanceof HttpError ? error : new HttpError(500, 'internal', describe(error));
       if (res.headersSent) {
         res.end();
         return;
       }
       sendError(res, http);
     });
   });
 
   return {
     listen: (port, host) => new Promise((resolveUrl, reject) => {
       server.once('error', reject);
       server.listen(port, host, () => {
         server.off('error', reject);
         const address = server.address();
         if (address === null || typeof address === 'string') {
           reject(new Error('api server: listening on something other than a TCP port'));
           return;
         }
         const shown = address.family === 'IPv6' ? `[${address.address}]` : address.address;
         resolveUrl(`http://${shown}:${String(address.port)}`);
       });
     }),
     close: async () => {
       for (const res of open) res.end();
       open.clear();
       await new Promise<void>((done) => {
         server.close(() => { done(); });
         server.closeAllConnections();
       });
       await Promise.all([...runs.values()].flatMap((entry) => (entry.drive === null ? [] : [entry.drive])));
     },
   };
 }

 /**
  * The line: stations 1-8 driven by the station machine. Each turn of the loop
  * asks `nextStep` what to do from committed state alone, does it, and commits
  * what happened, so a run resumed from any committed state takes the same next
  * step a run that never stopped would have taken.
  *
  * What each station does here:
  * - `intake` and `integrate`: nothing of their own at M1; their exit gates are
  *   the station. `integrate`'s merge is I1's.
  * - `spec`, `test-design`, `plan`: lock the artifacts the run was admitted
  *   with, and refuse a lock whose hash differs from the admission hash (I3).
+ *   A run admitted without its spec or its acceptance tests drafts them at
+ *   that station instead (R19, `drafting.ts`): a drafter writes, an
+ *   independent critic reads, the base run runs a test draft, and the exit's
+ *   approval carries the drafted floor; the approved bytes are written into
+ *   the artifact root and the base, and locked against the approved hash.
  *   `plan` also enters the graph's tasks into run state. `test-design`
  *   then runs every declared check over a fresh copy of the base, read-only
  *   and with no egress, records what each did, and refuses its exit unless
  *   each did what the manifest declares, before any driver is invoked
  *   (D-A-BF-01).
  * - `build`: runs each task through the driver over a fresh copy of the base
  *   and the diff accepted so far, never over the author's working copy, and
  *   records the result in the Vault before anything reads it (D-P6-02). Every
  *   task in the step's batch runs at once, each in its own sandbox, and every
  *   one is awaited and recorded before the line takes its next step (R4).
  * - `verify`: collects the task's own diff, refuses one that touches a locked
  *   artifact or leaves the task's grant, sends it back to `build` when a path
  *   in it was also changed by a sibling's work accepted after it started (a
  *   conflict, never resolved by picking a side), builds a tree from base and the
  *   cumulative diff, and runs every check over it in a fresh sandbox, read-only
  *   and with no egress. The verdict follows the check results alone (I2); the
  *   claim is diffed against the evidence and never decides anything. The
  *   tree the task was handed and the tree the checks ran over are read for
  *   tamper; each finding is recorded, carried in the bundle, and escalates
  *   `integrate`'s exit, and none decides the task's status.
  * - `review`: seats a reviewer against the authors' recorded model families
  *   (I6) and runs it on context its contract grants, over a view holding only
  *   the files those grants cover.
  *
  * Every driver call — each build attempt, a failed one included, and each
  * review seat — writes one `UsageRecord` from what the sandbox's relay counted,
  * read by `destroy` after the sandbox stopped (D-P13-08). What the driver says
  * it used is recorded in its result and is never the source of a cost.
  *
  * Locks are re-verified before every task step, after the checks, and on every
  * exit once anything is locked. A mismatch records a violation, and a run with
  * a recorded violation does not continue (I3).
  */

 /**
  * Drives the run until it completes or is refused. `changed` carries admitted
  * artifacts found altered before the line started, on resume; they are a
  * tamper like any other.
  */
 export async function runLine(ctx: LineContext, changed: readonly TamperedPath[] = []): Promise<RunOutcome> {
   if (changed.length > 0) {
     return refused(ctx, ctx.state.station, await recordTamper(ctx, ctx.state.station, null, [...changed], { phase: 'resume' }), null);
   }
   for (;;) {
     const cancel = ctx.state.cancelled === null ? (ctx.cancelRequested?.() ?? null) : null;
     if (cancel !== null) await commit(ctx, { cancelled: cancel });
     const step = nextStep(ctx.state, ctx.graph, stepLimits(ctx));
     let refusal: StationRefusal | undefined;
     // The task a refusal was made over, for its record; null for one made over the station.
     let task: TaskId | null = null;
     switch (step.kind) {
       case 'refuse':
         return refused(ctx, ctx.state.station, step.refusal, null);
       case 'work':
         refusal = await work(ctx, step.station);
         break;
       case 'build': {
         // Every task in the batch is awaited, whatever another did: a sibling already building
         // finishes, and its result and usage are recorded, before a refusal or an error ends the drive (R4).
         const settled = await Promise.allSettled(step.tasks.map((t) => build(ctx, t)));
         const thrown = settled.find((s): s is PromiseRejectedResult => s.status === 'rejected');
         if (thrown !== undefined) throw thrown.reason;
         for (const [i, s] of settled.entries()) {
           if (s.status === 'fulfilled' && s.value !== undefined) {
             task = step.tasks[i]?.id ?? null;
             refusal = s.value;
             break;
           }
         }
         break;
       }
       case 'verify':
         task = step.task.id;
         refusal = await verify(ctx, step.task);
         break;
       case 'review':
         task = step.task.id;
         refusal = await review(ctx, step.task);
         break;
       case 'finish':
         await commit(ctx, { phase: 'exiting' });
         break;
       case 'exit': {
-        const tampered = locksHeldLeaving(step.from) ? await tamperedPaths(ctx) : [];
+        // A drafted artifact's exit carries its draft and the conditions the runtime found failed (R19).
+        const floor = isDraftStation(step.from) && isDrafted(ctx, step.from) ? draftFloorOf(ctx.state, step.from) : undefined;
+        // A drafted spec locks nothing before its exit, so there is nothing yet to re-verify leaving it.
+        const tampered = locksHeldLeaving(step.from) && (await ctx.components.vault.readLocks(ctx.run.id)) !== null ? await tamperedPaths(ctx) : [];
         // Tamper findings escalate the gate `protectedPathPolicy` names, and only that one.
         const escalations = step.from === 'integrate' ? await integrateEscalations(ctx.components.vault, ctx.state, (ref) => readBundle(ctx, ref)) : NO_ESCALATIONS;
         const next = transition({
           from: step.from,
           to: step.to,
           level: ctx.run.requestedLevel,
           policy: ctx.policy,
           tampered,
           grants: ctx.state.approvals,
           protectedPathsTouched: escalations.protectedPathsTouched,
           tamperFindings: escalations.tamperFindings,
+          ...(floor === undefined ? {} : { draft: floor }),
         });
+        if (floor !== undefined && isDraftStation(step.from)) await recordFloor(ctx, step.from, floor);
         if (!next.ok) {
           refusal = next.reason === 'lock-tamper'
             ? await recordTamper(ctx, step.from, null, next.tampered, { phase: 'exit' })
             : next;
           break;
         }
+        const draft = floor?.draft ?? null;
+        // An approved draft is written and locked before the move, so the next station reads it (R19).
+        if (floor !== undefined && isDraftStation(step.from)) {
+          const grant = next.spends === null ? undefined : ctx.state.approvals.find((g) => grantClears(g, next.spends ?? g.key, draft));
+          const locked = await lockApproved(ctx, step.from, grant === undefined ? { by: 'policy' } : { by: 'principal', principal: grant.approvedBy });
+          if (locked !== undefined) {
+            refusal = locked;
+            break;
+          }
+        }
         // One human approval crosses one exit: the grant is spent in the same
         // commit as the move, so a stop between the two leaves it unspent and
         // the gate is evaluated again (A-P4-04).
-        const approvals = spend(ctx.state.approvals, next.spends);
+        const approvals = spend(ctx.state.approvals, next.spends, draft);
         // The run has passed its last M1 exit gate; `observe` is never entered at M1.
         if (!M1_STATIONS.includes(next.next)) {
           // Merged before the grant is spent: a stop between the two leaves the grant unspent, and the
           // resume finds the pull request merged at the same commit and records the same act (D-I1b-01).
           if (step.from === 'integrate') await mergeAccepted(ctx);
           if (next.spends !== null) await commit(ctx, { approvals });
           return { ok: true, state: ctx.state };
         }
         await commit(ctx, { station: next.next, phase: 'working', approvals });
         break;
       }
     }
     if (refusal !== undefined) return refused(ctx, ctx.state.station, refusal, task);
   }
 }
 
 /**
  * A refusal leaves the line only once it is recorded, naming the station
  * machine or the line as the decider (D-P14-07, D-P14-09). A park is recorded
  * over the task it parked, however the refusal reached here.
  */

  * and a stop between the two leaves a record of a park that did not take
  * effect, never the reverse (D-P14-07; external review of P14, codex-2).

 /** Every violation the line finds is written through the Vault and then recorded as its decision, pointing at it (D-P14-08). */
 async function recordViolation(ctx: LineContext, violation: IntegrityViolation): Promise<VaultRef> {

     { cause: 'violation-recorded', decidedBy: 'line', violation: ref },
   );

 /**

  * detail beside the tampered list, so a violation read back alone says when
  * it was found.

  * that task's doing, and passing null there falls back to the last task that
  * actually ran, or to `unattributed` (D-P4-08). The provenance recorded is the
  * detector's — the driver of the station the mismatch was found at — not

+export async function recordTamper(

 ): Promise<StationRefusal> {

     contractVersion: detector.contractVersion,

 /**
  * Starts an attempt at a task, counted and committed before the driver is
  * called. A task already `running` was in flight when the run stopped: the
  * attempt is run again, so it costs no iteration — but it is another
  * invocation, and `starts` counts it. Past the station's invocation bound the
  * task parks rather than being replayed into the same attempt forever
  * (A-P4-06).
  */
-async function startAttempt(ctx: LineContext, task: Task): Promise<StationRefusal | undefined> {
+export async function startAttempt(ctx: LineContext, task: Task): Promise<StationRefusal | undefined> {
   const contract = STATION_CONTRACTS[ctx.state.station];
   const spent = attemptsOf(ctx.state, task);
   const replay = statusOf(ctx.state, task) === 'running';
   const attempt: TaskAttempts = replay
     ? { ...spent, starts: spent.starts + 1 }
     : { iterations: spent.iterations + 1, retries: 0, starts: spent.starts + 1, conflicts: spent.conflicts };
   if (attempt.starts > maxStarts(contract, spent.conflicts.length)) {
     return park(ctx, task, { attempts: { [task.id]: spent } }, 'starts-exhausted');
   }
   // An escalation is a decision, recorded before the attempt that carries it is committed, as a park
   // is: a stop between the two leaves a record of an escalation that did not take effect, and the
   // resume decides it again from the same counts (A-R14-02, D-P14-07). A replay is the same iteration.
   const escalation = replay ? null : escalationAt(scopeFor(ctx, task), ctx.state.station, attempt);
   if (escalation !== null) {
     await recordDecision(
       { vault: ctx.components.vault, runId: ctx.run.id, taskId: task.id, station: ctx.state.station },
       { cause: 'tier-escalated', decidedBy: 'line', ...escalation },
     );
   }
   await commit(ctx, {
     tasks: { [task.id]: 'running' },
     attempts: { [task.id]: attempt },
   });
   return undefined;
 }
 
 /**
  * A driver or sandbox failure: one retry spent, committed before the next
  * attempt. Past the station's `retry.max` the task parks and the run stops.

-async function spendRetry(ctx: LineContext, task: Task): Promise<StationRefusal | undefined> {
+export async function spendRetry(ctx: LineContext, task: Task): Promise<StationRefusal | undefined> {
   const contract = STATION_CONTRACTS[ctx.state.station];
   const spent = attemptsOf(ctx.state, task);
   const attempts = { [task.id]: { ...spent, retries: spent.retries + 1 } };
   if (spent.retries + 1 > contract.retry.max) {
     return park(ctx, task, { attempts }, 'retries-exhausted');
   }
   await commit(ctx, { attempts });
   await new Promise((done) => setTimeout(done, contract.retry.backoffMs));
   return undefined;

 /**
  * Reads admitted artifacts for a model's context and hashes the bytes it read,
  * not the path it read them from. `verifyLocks` is a point-in-time check of a
  * mutable path, so a swap between that check and this read would otherwise
  * reach the model with every lock comparison still intact; what is hashed here
  * is exactly what is handed over (A-P4-05).
  */
-async function readText(ctx: LineContext, artifacts: readonly AdmittedArtifact[]): Promise<ReadArtifacts> {
+export async function readText(ctx: LineContext, artifacts: readonly AdmittedArtifact[]): Promise<ReadArtifacts> {
   const texts: string[] = [];
   const tampered: TamperedPath[] = [];
   for (const artifact of artifacts) {
     let bytes: Uint8Array;
     try {
       bytes = await readFile(resolve(ctx.run.repo, artifact.path));
     } catch {
       tampered.push({ path: artifact.path, expected: artifact.sha256, actual: 'missing' });
       continue;
     }
     const actual = createHash('sha256').update(bytes).digest('hex');
     if (actual !== artifact.sha256) tampered.push({ path: artifact.path, expected: artifact.sha256, actual });
     texts.push(decode(bytes));
   }
   return { text: texts.join('\n'), tampered };
 }

  * agent never ran, mounted read-only with no egress. The verdict follows the

 async function verify(ctx: LineContext, task: Task): Promise<StationRefusal | undefined> {
   const { sandbox, vault, driver, workspaces } = ctx.components;
   const before = await tamperedPaths(ctx);
   if (before.length > 0) return recordTamper(ctx, 'verify', task, before, { phase: 'before-checks' });
   const result = await readResult(ctx, task);
 
   const { start, work } = attemptPaths(workspaces, ctx.run.id, task.id, attemptsOf(ctx.state, task).iterations);
   const written = await ownDiff(start, work);
   // D-R15-05: what the task wrote under node_modules is recorded and escalated, never carried. Every
   // check, and the runner a suite check starts, runs over the base's dependency trees.
   const own = written.filter((entry) => !inDependencyTree(entry.path));
   const notCarried = [...new Set(written.filter((entry) => inDependencyTree(entry.path)).map((entry) => dependencyEntry(entry.path)))].sort();
 
   // I3: the task's copy is not where the Vault checks the locks, so its diff is checked instead.
   // A locked path in it is the tamper the Vault's own check can no longer see.
   const admitted = new Map(admittedArtifacts(ctx).map((a) => [a.path, a.sha256]));
   const locked = own.filter((entry) => admitted.has(entry.path));

   const conflict = await conflictOf(ctx, task, start, own, prior);

   for (const [index, check] of specs.entries()) {

         const exec = await sandbox.exec(handle, [...commandOf(check, runner)]);

       const { egress } = await sandbox.destroy(handle);

     const shortfall = isSecurityCheck(check)

       : requiredShortfall(check, results[i]);

   });

   if (differences.length > 0) {
     // Recorded, and deliberately not entered in run state: every entry there halts the run, and a
     // halt the claim could trigger would let the model's story decide the outcome (D-P6-03).
     await recordViolation(ctx, {

     taskId: task.id,
     baseCommit: ctx.run.baseCommit,
     baseTreeSha256: ctx.baseTreeSha256,
     baseRun,
     diff,
     diffSha256,
     checks,
     unstarted,
     claim: result.claim,
     claimEvidenceDiff: differences,
     tamper,
     notCarried,
     collectedBy: 'runtime',
     driverProvenanceId: driver.provenanceId(),
     contractVersion: driver.contractVersion,
   };
   const ref = await vault.writeEvidence(evidence);
   // The status follows the verdict and nothing else (I2). A failure with no iteration left parks the task;
   // an iteration a conflict sent back was judged by no gate and spends none of them (D-R4-03).
   const spent = gatedIterations(attemptsOf(ctx.state, task));
   const status: TaskStatus = failed.length === 0 ? 'passed' : spent >= STATION_CONTRACTS[task.station].maxIterations ? 'parked' : 'failed';
   // A failed gate is a decision, recorded before the status it sets is committed, at every iteration, so each failure is counted by its cause (R17, D-R17-06).
   if (failed.length > 0) {

       { cause: 'station-refused', decidedBy: 'line', refusal: { ok: false, reason: 'gate-failed', failed, message: `the gate failed on ${failed.map((f) => `${f.checkId} (${f.cause})`).join(', ')}` } },
     );
   }
   // The station machine returns this park to the line on its next step; it is recorded here, first.
   if (status === 'parked') await park(ctx, task, { evidence: ref }, 'iterations-exhausted');
   else await commit(ctx, { tasks: { [task.id]: status }, evidence: ref });

 async function review(ctx: LineContext, task: Task): Promise<StationRefusal | undefined> {
   const { reviewer, vault } = ctx.components;
   const contract = STATION_CONTRACTS.review;
   const capability = capabilityRefusal(contract, reviewer.capabilities());

   const tests = await readText(ctx, ctx.artifacts.acceptanceTests);
   const drifted = [...spec.tampered, ...tests.tampered];

     'author-narrative': authored.map((r) => r.claim.narrative).join('\n'),
     plan: JSON.stringify(ctx.graph.tasks),
   };

   const ran = await runTask(ctx, task, reviewer, pinnedRole(ctx, REVIEWER_ROLE), joinParts(grantedContext(contract, offered)), await reviewView(ctx, task));

   const problems = taskResultProblems(ran.result);

     throw new Error(`line: the reviewer's result for task ${task.id} does not match the TaskResult contract, so it was not recorded: ${problems.join('; ')}`);

   });

 /** Reads one run's records. A run with no state is one refused at admission, or one with nothing yet. */
 export async function readRunRecords(vault: Vault, runId: RunId): Promise<RunRecords> {
   const decisions: EnforcementDecision[] = [];

   // after a merge was seen holds that grant, and a merge is never paired with an older state.
   const integration: IntegrationRecord[] = await readIntegration(vault, runId);
 
   let state: RunState;

     state = await vault.readRunState(runId);
   } catch (error) {
     // Only absence is absence; a state that cannot be read is a fault, never a run with no evidence (I5).

     // the last carries the task's status, the runtime's verdict and nothing else (I2).

     }));

     stack: recordedStack(admission),
     startedAt: admission.run.createdAt,
