+const GRADE_ORDER: readonly string[] = ['different-family', 'different-model', 'reduced', 'same-model'];
+
+/** The weakest grade among the run's review seats. A seat recorded before grades reads `independent` as another family, and `reduced` as itself. */
+function reviewGradeOf(state: RunState): string {
+  let weakest: string | null = null;
+  for (const seat of state.reviews) {
+    const raw = seat.independence as string;
+    const grade = raw === 'independent' ? 'different-family' : raw;
+    if (weakest === null || GRADE_ORDER.indexOf(grade) > GRADE_ORDER.indexOf(weakest)) weakest = grade;
+  }
+  return weakest ?? 'no-review';
+}

+/** Every critic result the run recorded, by grade, from the attempts run state holds (R19). */
+async function critiquesOf(vault: Vault, state: RunState): Promise<RunRecords['critiques']> {
+  const critiques: Array<{ grade: string; found: boolean }> = [];
+  for (const drafts of Object.values(state.drafts)) {

+      if (attempt.critic === null || attempt.critic === 'not-seated') continue;
+      const critic = decode(await vault.read(attempt.critic)) as CriticRecord;
+      critiques.push({ grade: critic.grade, found: critic.findings.length > 0 });
+    }
+  }
+  return critiques;

 /** The derivation itself, over records already read: pure, so a figure is a function of its records. */
 export function deriveOutcomes(runs: readonly RunRecords[], window: OutcomeWindow = {}): Outcomes {
   const population: Record<RunEnding, number> = { refused: 0, merged: 0, passed: 0, cancelled: 0, halted: 0, parked: 0, unfinished: 0 };
   for (const run of runs) population[run.ending] += 1;
 
   // Safety: every attempt, finished run or not — an attempt the runtime judged is a fact either way.
   let attempts = 0;
   let falseDone = 0;
   const fdStation = new Tally();
   const fdModel = new Tally();
   const fdLevel = new Tally();
   const fdStack = new Tally();
+  const fdGrade = new Tally();
   for (const run of runs) {
     for (const task of run.tasks) {
       const model = task.models.length > 1 ? 'escalated' : (task.models[0] ?? 'no-call');
       for (const a of task.attempts) {
         const bad = a.outcome !== 'passed' || a.claimMismatch;
         attempts += 1;
         if (bad) falseDone += 1;
         fdStation.add(task.station ?? 'no-call', bad);
         fdModel.add(model, bad);
         fdLevel.add(`L${String(run.requestedLevel)}`, bad);
         fdStack.add(stackKey(run), bad);
+        fdGrade.add(run.reviewGrade, bad);
       }
     }
   }
 
   // Throughput over admitted runs that finished; an unfinished run is counted in the population and nowhere else.
   const finished = runs.filter((r) => r.ending !== 'refused' && r.ending !== 'unfinished');
   const autonomous = (r: RunRecords): boolean => r.ending === 'merged' && r.approvalsSpent === 0;
   const byLevel = new Tally();
   const byStack = new Tally();
+  const byGrade = new Tally();
   for (const r of finished) {
     byLevel.add(`L${String(r.requestedLevel)}`, autonomous(r));
     byStack.add(stackKey(r), autonomous(r));
+    byGrade.add(r.reviewGrade, autonomous(r));
   }
+  const critics = new Tally();
+  for (const run of runs) for (const c of run.critiques) critics.add(c.grade, c.found);
 
   const fpStation = new Tally();
   const fpModel = new Tally();
   const fpStack = new Tally();
   const toGreen: number[] = [];
   const perTask: number[] = [];
   for (const run of runs) {
     for (const task of run.tasks) {
       const first = task.attempts[0];
       if (first === undefined) continue;
       fpStation.add(task.station ?? 'no-call', first.outcome === 'passed');
       fpModel.add(task.firstModel ?? 'no-call', first.outcome === 'passed');
       fpStack.add(stackKey(run), first.outcome === 'passed');
       perTask.push(task.attempts.length);
       if (task.status === 'passed') toGreen.push(task.iterations);
     }
   }
   const yields = fpStation.rates(successRate);
   const rolledYield = Object.keys(yields).length === 0 || Object.values(yields).some((p) => p.kind === 'no-sample')
     ? null
     : Object.values(yields).reduce((product, p) => product * (p.kind === 'rate' ? p.rate : 1), 1);
 
   let seated = 0;
   let returned = 0;
   const decisionsByCause: Record<string, number> = {};
   const refusalsByReason: Record<string, number> = {};
   const parksByCause: Record<string, number> = {};
   const waits = new Map<string, number[]>();
   const open: Record<string, number> = {};
   for (const run of runs) {
     // A run-level rate, so over finished runs: a review that has not returned is not a sample.
     if (finished.includes(run) && run.usage.some((u) => u.station === 'review')) {
       seated += 1;
       if (run.decisions.some((d) => d.station === 'review' && d.decision.cause === 'station-refused' && d.decision.refusal.reason !== 'approval-required')) returned += 1;
     }
     const requested = new Map<string, string[]>();
     for (const d of run.decisions) {
       bump(decisionsByCause, d.decision.cause);
       if (d.decision.cause === 'station-refused') {
         bump(refusalsByReason, d.decision.refusal.reason);
         if (d.decision.refusal.reason === 'parked') bump(parksByCause, d.decision.refusal.cause);
         if (d.decision.refusal.reason === 'approval-required') requested.set(d.decision.refusal.key, [...(requested.get(d.decision.refusal.key) ?? []), d.decidedAt]);
       }
     }
     // Each grant clears the first request of its key made before it; the requests repeated by resumes before that grant are the same wait.
     for (const [key, times] of requested) {
       const grants = run.decisions.filter((d) => d.decision.cause === 'approval-granted' && d.decision.key === key).map((d) => d.decidedAt);
       let from = 0;
       for (const granted of grants) {
         const first = times.slice(from).find((t) => t <= granted);
         if (first === undefined) continue;
         waits.set(key, [...(waits.get(key) ?? []), Date.parse(granted) - Date.parse(first)]);
         from = times.findIndex((t) => t > granted);
         if (from === -1) from = times.length;
       }
       if (from < times.length) bump(open, key);
     }
   }
 
   const merges = runs.filter((r) => r.ending === 'merged');
   const cycle = merges.flatMap((r) => (r.startedAt === null || r.mergedAt === null ? [] : [Date.parse(r.mergedAt) - Date.parse(r.startedAt)]));
   const spent = runs.reduce((sum, r) => sum + (r.cost?.metered.costUsd ?? 0), 0);
   const unknown = runs.some((r) => r.cost !== null && r.cost.bound === 'lower');
 
   return {
     window,
     population: { ...population, total: runs.length },
     safety: {
       caughtFalseDone: failureRate(falseDone, attempts),
       caughtFalseDoneByStation: fdStation.rates(failureRate),
       caughtFalseDoneByModel: fdModel.rates(failureRate),
       caughtFalseDoneByLevel: fdLevel.rates(failureRate),
       caughtFalseDoneByStack: fdStack.rates(failureRate),
+      caughtFalseDoneByGrade: fdGrade.rates(failureRate),
       escapedFalseDone: 'not-established',
     },
     throughput: {
       autonomyRatio: successRate(finished.filter(autonomous).length, finished.length),
       autonomyRatioByLevel: byLevel.rates(successRate),
       autonomyRatioByStack: byStack.rates(successRate),
+      autonomyRatioByGrade: byGrade.rates(successRate),
+      criticFindingsByGrade: critics.rates(failureRate),
       firstPassYieldByStation: yields,
       firstPassYieldByModel: fpModel.rates(successRate),
       firstPassYieldByStack: fpStack.rates(successRate),
       rolledYield,
       iterationsToGreen: distribution(toGreen),
       attemptsPerTask: distribution(perTask),
       reviewExitRefused: failureRate(returned, seated),
       decisionsByCause,
       refusalsByReason,
       parksByCause,
       approvalWait: Object.fromEntries([...waits.entries()].sort(([a], [b]) => a.localeCompare(b)).map(([k, v]) => [k, distribution(v)])),
       approvalsOpen: open,
       cycleTime: distribution(cycle),
       costPerMergedChange: merges.length === 0
         ? { kind: 'no-sample', spentUsd: spent }
         : { kind: 'cost', usd: spent / merges.length, spentUsd: spent, merges: merges.length, bound: unknown ? 'lower' : 'exact' },
     },
     notMeasured: NOT_MEASURED,
   };
 }

===== packages/api/src/rubrics.ts =====
/**
 * The rubrics a person writes for the critics at `spec` and `test-design`
 * (R19). One Markdown file per drafting station, `spec.md` and
 * `test-design.md`, in a directory on the host beside policy and the roles,
 * which no sandbox mounts. Read at admission for each station the run drafts,
 * recorded in the Vault, and kept for the run: a rubric edited later changes
 * later runs, never this one.
 *
 * The rubric judges, so nothing proposes changes to it (R11 never does), and
 * it never enters a role: the role compiler takes no rubric (D-A-RC-05). It
 * reaches a critic as a granted context part and nowhere else.
 *
 * Every limit is a refusal naming the file (I5): one that is not a regular
 * file, is over the byte cap, is not UTF-8, or holds nothing.
 */
import { createHash } from 'node:crypto';
import { lstat, readFile, realpath } from 'node:fs/promises';
import { isAbsolute, join, resolve } from 'node:path';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
import type { DraftStation } from '@olympus-ai/core';
import type { RequestProblem } from './validate.js';
import { within } from './workspace.js';

/** The rubrics shipped with the runtime, a starting point a person edits, as `DEFAULT_ROLES_DIRECTORY` is for roles. */
export const DEFAULT_RUBRICS_DIRECTORY: string = fileURLToPath(new URL('../rubrics/', import.meta.url));

/** The most bytes a rubric may hold, as a role may (D-R20-03): it reaches a critic in its prompt. */
export const RUBRIC_MAX_BYTES = 32 * 1024;

export interface ReadRubric {
  readonly text: string;
  readonly sha256: string;
}

function problem(station: DraftStation, message: string): RequestProblem {
  return { path: `rubrics.${station}`, code: 'unseated', message };
}

/** Each drafting station's rubric, read from the bytes on disk and hashed, or every problem found. */
export async function readRubrics(
  directory: string, stations: readonly DraftStation[],
): Promise<{ ok: true; rubrics: Partial<Record<DraftStation, ReadRubric>> } | { ok: false; problems: RequestProblem[] }> {
  const problems: RequestProblem[] = [];
  const rubrics: Partial<Record<DraftStation, ReadRubric>> = {};
  for (const station of stations) {
    const file = join(directory, `${station}.md`);
    const facts = await lstat(file).catch(() => null);
    if (facts === null) {
      problems.push(problem(station, `the rubrics directory holds no ${station}.md, and a ${station} critic is never seated without the rubric a person wrote`));
      continue;
    }
    if (!facts.isFile()) {
      problems.push(problem(station, `${station}.md is not a regular file`));
      continue;
    }
    if (facts.size > RUBRIC_MAX_BYTES) {
      problems.push(problem(station, `${station}.md is ${String(facts.size)} bytes, over the ${String(RUBRIC_MAX_BYTES)} a rubric may hold`));
      continue;
    }
    const bytes = await readFile(file);
    let text: string;
    try {
      text = new TextDecoder('utf-8', { fatal: true }).decode(bytes);
    } catch {
      problems.push(problem(station, `${station}.md is not UTF-8`));
      continue;
    }
    if (text.trim() === '') {
      problems.push(problem(station, `${station}.md holds no rubric`));
      continue;
    }
    rubrics[station] = { text, sha256: createHash('sha256').update(bytes).digest('hex') };
  }
  return problems.length === 0 ? { ok: true, rubrics } : { ok: false, problems };
}

async function resolved(path: string): Promise<string> {
  return realpath(path).catch(() => resolve(path));
}

/**
 * The rubrics directory may lie inside neither the workspace nor the
 * workspace store, nor hold either, compared with every link resolved: a
 * rubric a drafter could reach is one it could rewrite to judge itself by
 * (I3), as a role there is one an agent could rewrite (D-R20-04).
 */
export async function rubricsProblems(directory: unknown, places: ReadonlyArray<readonly [string, string]>): Promise<RequestProblem[]> {
  if (typeof directory !== 'string' || directory === '' || !isAbsolute(directory)) {
    return [{ path: 'rubrics', code: 'not-absolute', message: 'rubrics must be the absolute path of the rubrics directory' }];
  }
  const rubrics = await resolved(directory);
  for (const [name, place] of places) {
    const outer = await resolved(place);
    if (within(outer, rubrics) || within(rubrics, outer)) {
      return [{ path: 'rubrics', code: 'overlaps', message: `the rubrics directory ${directory} and the ${name} ${place} overlap; rubrics are read from the host, outside every tree a sandbox mounts` }];
    }
  }
  return [];
}

+/** The stations a run drafts at, in line order. */
+function draftedStations(drafted: Readonly<Record<DraftStation, boolean>>): DraftStation[] {
+  return DRAFT_STATIONS.filter((station) => drafted[station]);
+}
+
+/**
+ * Why a run's drafting cannot be admitted (R19): an intent where none is
+ * drafted from or none where one is, drafting drivers missing, a drafts
+ * directory the workspace already holds, and a test drafter or critic
+ * granted egress, which would let it reach what the black box keeps from it.
+ */
+function draftingProblems(req: RunRequest, drafted: Readonly<Record<DraftStation, boolean>>, policy: Policy): RequestProblem[] {
+  const problems: RequestProblem[] = [];
+  const intent: unknown = req.intent ?? null;
+  if (drafted.spec) {
+    const i = intent as Partial<Intent> | null;
+    if (i === null) problems.push({ path: 'intent', code: 'missing', message: 'a run that drafts its spec is drafted from a person\'s intent, and none was given' });
+    else if (typeof i !== 'object' || typeof i.text !== 'string' || i.text.trim() === '' || i.from !== 'principal' || Object.keys(i).length !== 2) {
+      problems.push({ path: 'intent', code: 'not-an-intent', message: 'an intent is a person\'s text from the principal\'s channel: { text, from: \'principal\' }' });
+    } else if (Buffer.byteLength(i.text, 'utf8') > INTENT_MAX_BYTES) {
+      problems.push({ path: 'intent', code: 'not-an-intent', message: `an intent is at most ${String(INTENT_MAX_BYTES)} bytes` });
+    }
+  } else if (intent !== null) {
+    problems.push({ path: 'intent', code: 'not-applicable', message: 'an intent is what a spec is drafted from; a run admitted with its spec takes none' });
+  }
+  const stations = draftedStations(drafted);
+  if (stations.length > 0 && (req.components.drafting ?? null) === null) {
+    problems.push({ path: 'components.drafting', code: 'unseated', message: 'a run that drafts needs the drivers that seat each drafter and each critic; none is inferred from the build or review driver' });
+  }
+  if (stations.length > 0 && (req.rubrics ?? null) === null) {
+    problems.push({ path: 'rubrics', code: 'unseated', message: 'a run that drafts needs the rubrics its critics check drafts against' });
+  }
+  if (drafted['test-design']) {
+    for (const seat of ['drafter', 'critic'] as const) {
+      const role = DRAFT_ROLES['test-design'][seat];
+      const scope = Object.hasOwn(policy.roles, role) ? policy.roles[role] : undefined;
+      const egress = scope?.network.egress;
+      if (egress !== undefined && egress !== 'none' && egress.length > 0) {
+        problems.push({ path: `policy.roles.${role}.network.egress`, code: 'not-black-box', message: `the ${role} is black-box in fact: it reaches the model through its relay and nothing else, so its egress is none` });
+      }
+    }
+  }
+  return problems;
+}
+

+const INTENT_MAX_BYTES = 32 * 1024;

-/** A record's `specParts`: an object whose security part is an admitted artifact or null. Read from a record, so its type is not trusted. */
-function specPartsShaped(parts: unknown): boolean {
-  if (typeof parts !== 'object' || parts === null) return false;
-  const { security } = parts as { security?: unknown };
-  return security === null || typeof security === 'object';
+function isRecordLike(value: unknown): value is Record<string, unknown> {
+  return typeof value === 'object' && value !== null && !Array.isArray(value);
+}
+
+function isAdmittedArtifact(value: unknown): value is AdmittedArtifact {
+  return isRecordLike(value) && typeof value.path === 'string' && typeof value.sha256 === 'string';
+}
+
+function nullableArtifact(value: unknown): AdmittedArtifact | null | undefined {
+  if (value === null || value === undefined) return null;
+  return isAdmittedArtifact(value) ? value : undefined;
+}
+
+/**
+ * A record's artifacts in today's shape, or undefined where they are not
+ * artifacts at all. Read from a record, so its type is not trusted. A record
+ * written before R19 holds lists, read as supplied sets, no acceptance
+ * manifest, and a spec with only its security part.
+ */
+function recordArtifacts(artifacts: unknown): AdmissionRecord['artifacts'] | undefined {
+  if (!isRecordLike(artifacts)) return undefined;
+  const set = (value: unknown): AdmittedSet | undefined => {
+    if (Array.isArray(value)) return value.every(isAdmittedArtifact) ? { source: 'supplied', files: value } : undefined;
+    if (isRecordLike(value) && value.source === 'drafted') return { source: 'drafted' };
+    if (isRecordLike(value) && value.source === 'supplied' && Array.isArray(value.files) && value.files.every(isAdmittedArtifact)) return { source: 'supplied', files: value.files };
+    return undefined;
+  };
+  const manifest = ((value: unknown): AdmittedManifest | undefined => {
+    if (value === undefined) return { source: 'none' };
+    if (isRecordLike(value) && (value.source === 'none' || value.source === 'drafted')) return { source: value.source };
+    if (isRecordLike(value) && value.source === 'supplied' && isAdmittedArtifact(value.file)) return { source: 'supplied', file: value.file };
+    return undefined;
+  })(artifacts.acceptanceManifest);
+  const spec = set(artifacts.spec);
+  const tests = set(artifacts.acceptanceTests);
+  const parts = artifacts.specParts;
+  if (spec === undefined || tests === undefined || manifest === undefined || !isRecordLike(parts)) return undefined;
+  const security = nullableArtifact(parts.security);
+  const runRequirements = nullableArtifact(parts.runRequirements);
+  const questions = nullableArtifact(parts.questions);
+  if (!isAdmittedArtifact(artifacts.verificationManifest) || !isAdmittedArtifact(artifacts.taskGraph) || security === undefined || runRequirements === undefined || questions === undefined) return undefined;
+  return {
+    spec,
+    acceptanceTests: tests,
+    acceptanceManifest: manifest,
+    verificationManifest: artifacts.verificationManifest,
+    taskGraph: artifacts.taskGraph,
+    specParts: { security, runRequirements, questions },
+  };
+}
+
+/** The line's artifacts as admitted: supplied ones as they are, drafted ones empty until locked (R19). */
+export function lineArtifactsOf(artifacts: AdmissionRecord['artifacts']): LineArtifacts {
+  return {
+    spec: artifacts.spec.source === 'supplied' ? artifacts.spec.files : [],
+    acceptanceTests: artifacts.acceptanceTests.source === 'supplied' ? artifacts.acceptanceTests.files : [],
+    acceptanceManifest: artifacts.acceptanceManifest.source === 'supplied' ? artifacts.acceptanceManifest.file : null,
+    verificationManifest: artifacts.verificationManifest,
+    taskGraph: artifacts.taskGraph,
+    specParts: artifacts.specParts,
+  };
 }

  * it was admitted at, and the effective approval there must be
  * `human-required`: a `blocked` exit cannot be approved, an `auto` one needs no

+const REJECTION_REASONS: readonly RejectionReason[] = ['not-black-box', 'does-not-follow-spec', 'not-runnable', 'misses-spec-cases'];

+/**
+ * Records a person's rejection of the draft waiting at a drafting station's
+ * exit (R19). The draft goes back to the drafter with the notes at `spec` or
+ * the reason at `test-design`; a rejection of the last allowed attempt parks
+ * the run, terminally. Recorded before it takes effect, as an approval is
+ * (D-P14-07).
+ */
+export async function rejectDraft(req: RejectionRequest): Promise<RejectionOutcome> {
+  const key = isApprovalKey(req.key) ? req.key : null;
+  const invalid = (message: string): RejectionOutcome => ({ ok: false, reason: 'invalid-request', message });
+  if (typeof req.rejectedBy !== 'string' || req.rejectedBy.trim() === '') return invalid('rejectedBy must name who rejected');
+  if (key === null) return invalid(`'${req.key}' is not a station:level approval key`);
+  if (typeof req.draft !== 'string') return invalid('draft must be the hash of the draft rejected');
+  const notes: unknown = req.notes ?? null;
+  const reason: unknown = req.reason ?? null;
+  const state = await req.vault.readRunState(req.runId);
+  const { record, policy } = await readAdmission(req.vault, state);
+  const level = record.run.requestedLevel;
+  const { graph, changed, drafting } = await loadLine(req.vault, record, state);
+  const station = state.station;
+  if (changed.length > 0 || !isDraftStation(station)) return { ok: false, reason: 'not-awaiting', message: `run ${req.runId} is not waiting at a drafted exit` };
+  const step = nextStep(state, graph, policy.concurrency);
+  const floor = step.kind === 'exit' ? floorAt(state, drafting) : undefined;
+  if (floor === undefined || key !== approvalKey(station, level) || effectiveApproval(STATION_CONTRACTS[station], policy, level, [], floor) !== 'human-required') {
+    return { ok: false, reason: 'not-awaiting', message: `run ${req.runId} is not waiting for a person to decide a draft at ${key}` };
+  }
+  if (floor.draft.sha256 !== req.draft) return { ok: false, reason: 'draft-mismatch', message: `the draft waiting at ${station} is ${floor.draft.sha256}, not ${req.draft}` };
+  // At `spec`, notes and no reason; at `test-design`, a reason from the fixed set and no text (D-A-DS-02).
+  if (station === 'spec') {
+    if (reason !== null) return invalid('a rejection at spec carries notes, not a reason');
+    if (notes !== null && (typeof notes !== 'string' || notes.trim() === '' || Buffer.byteLength(notes, 'utf8') > INTENT_MAX_BYTES)) return invalid(`notes are the approver's text, at most ${String(INTENT_MAX_BYTES)} bytes`);
+  } else {
+    if (notes !== null) return invalid('a rejection at test-design carries no free text; an approver who has read the repository would otherwise be a channel around the black box');
+    if (typeof reason !== 'string' || !REJECTION_REASONS.includes(reason as RejectionReason)) return invalid(`a rejection at test-design carries a reason: one of ${REJECTION_REASONS.join(', ')}`);
+  }
+  const drafts = state.drafts[station];
+  const last = drafts?.attempts.at(-1);
+  if (drafts === undefined || last === undefined) return { ok: false, reason: 'not-awaiting', message: `run ${req.runId} has no draft at ${station}` };
+  const at = new Date().toISOString();
+  const parks = last.attempt >= STATION_CONTRACTS[station].maxIterations;
+  await recordDecision(
+    { vault: req.vault, runId: req.runId, taskId: null, station },
+    { cause: 'approval-rejected', decidedBy: 'approval', key, draft: floor.draft, principal: req.rejectedBy, reason: (reason as RejectionReason | null), notes: notes !== null },
+  );
+  // The last allowed draft rejected parks the run; the park is recorded with its cause before it is committed (D-P14-07).
+  if (parks) {
+    const parked = draftsRejected(station);
+    await recordDecision({ vault: req.vault, runId: req.runId, taskId: null, station }, { cause: 'station-refused', decidedBy: 'line', refusal: parked });
+  }
+  const sentBack = { by: 'principal' as const, principal: req.rejectedBy, at, notes: notes, reason: reason as RejectionReason | null };
+  try {
+    const next = await req.vault.commitRunState(
+      {
+        ...state,
+        // A draft sent back is drafted again: the station is working, until the last is rejected and the run parks.
+        phase: parks ? state.phase : 'working',
+        drafts: { ...state.drafts, [station]: { ...drafts, parked: parks, attempts: [...drafts.attempts.slice(0, -1), { ...last, sentBack }] } },
+      },
+      state.version,
+    );
+    return { ok: true, state: next, parked: parks };
+  } catch (error) {
+    return { ok: false, reason: 'not-awaiting', message: `run ${req.runId} moved while the rejection was recorded: ${describe(error)}` };
+  }
+}

+export interface DraftStanding {

===== packages/api/src/spec-parts.ts =====
/**
 * The two spec parts R19 adds beside the security part (R17): what the
 * acceptance checks need to run, and the drafter's assumptions and open
 * questions. Each is a JSON file locked with the spec. A drafted spec carries
 * both; a supplied spec may, and must carry the run requirements when its
 * acceptance tests are drafted, since they are the test drafter's only view of
 * the repository.
 *
 * Parsed here from the bytes the runtime read, so the part a station reads is
 * the part that was locked. Nothing here is a model's account of anything: a
 * part that does not parse is refused or sent back, never repaired (I5).
 */
import { isAbsolute, posix, win32 } from 'node:path';
import type { RequestProblem } from './validate.js';

/** Where every draft of a run lives in the tree, beside the repository's own files (R19). */
export const DRAFT_DIR = '.drafts';

/**
 * What the acceptance checks need to run, written by whoever wrote the spec:
 * the command a person runs the tests with, the framework, where the tests
 * live, and the public interface a check calls (R19).
 */
export interface RunRequirements {
  readonly testCommand: readonly [string, ...string[]];
  readonly framework: string;
  /** Repository-relative: where the acceptance tests live, so the framework finds them. */
  readonly testDirectory: string;
  readonly publicInterface: string;
}

/** Each point the intent left undecided and what the drafter chose, and each question it could not settle (R19). */
export interface Questions {
  readonly assumptions: ReadonlyArray<{ readonly point: string; readonly chose: string }>;
  readonly openQuestions: readonly string[];
}

function isRecord(value: unknown): value is Readonly<Record<string, unknown>> {
  return typeof value === 'object' && value !== null && !Array.isArray(value);
}

function isText(value: unknown): value is string {
  return typeof value === 'string' && value.trim() !== '';
}

function extraKeys(value: Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>, allowed: readonly string[]): string[] {
  return Object.keys(value).filter((key) => !allowed.includes(key));
}

/**
 * Whether `path` is a repository-relative directory a draft may be written
 * under: relative, inside the tree, not the tree itself, and not the drafts
 * directory, where nothing a framework runs belongs.
 */
export function isDraftableDirectory(path: string): boolean {
  if (path.trim() === '' || isAbsolute(path) || win32.isAbsolute(path) || path.includes('\\')) return false;
  const normal = posix.normalize(path).replace(/\/+$/, '');
  if (normal === '.' || normal === '' || normal.startsWith('../') || normal === '..') return false;
  const first = normal.split('/')[0];
  return first !== DRAFT_DIR && first !== '.git';
}

export function parseRunRequirements(value: unknown, at: string): { ok: true; part: RunRequirements } | { ok: false; problems: RequestProblem[] } {
  const problems: RequestProblem[] = [];
  const report = (path: string, message: string): void => {
    problems.push({ path, code: 'not-a-spec-part', message });
  };
  if (!isRecord(value)) {
    report(at, 'the run-requirements part is an object with testCommand, framework, testDirectory, and publicInterface');
    return { ok: false, problems };
  }
  for (const key of extraKeys(value, ['testCommand', 'framework', 'testDirectory', 'publicInterface'])) report(`${at}.${key}`, `'${key}' is not a field of the run-requirements part`);
  const command = value.testCommand;
  if (!Array.isArray(command) || command.length === 0 || !command.every(isText)) report(`${at}.testCommand`, 'testCommand is an argument vector: a non-empty list of non-empty strings');
  if (!isText(value.framework)) report(`${at}.framework`, 'framework names the test framework');
  if (!isText(value.testDirectory) || !isDraftableDirectory(value.testDirectory)) {
    report(`${at}.testDirectory`, `testDirectory is a repository-relative directory inside the tree, and not ${DRAFT_DIR}/ or .git/`);
  }
  if (!isText(value.publicInterface)) report(`${at}.publicInterface`, 'publicInterface says what a check calls');
  if (problems.length > 0) return { ok: false, problems };
  return {
    ok: true,
    part: {
      testCommand: [...(command as [string, ...string[]])],
      framework: value.framework as string,
      testDirectory: posix.normalize(value.testDirectory as string).replace(/\/+$/, ''),
      publicInterface: value.publicInterface as string,
    },
  };
}

export function parseQuestions(value: unknown, at: string): { ok: true; part: Questions } | { ok: false; problems: RequestProblem[] } {
  const problems: RequestProblem[] = [];
  const report = (path: string, message: string): void => {
    problems.push({ path, code: 'not-a-spec-part', message });
  };
  if (!isRecord(value)) {
    report(at, 'the questions part is an object with assumptions and openQuestions');
    return { ok: false, problems };
  }
  for (const key of extraKeys(value, ['assumptions', 'openQuestions'])) report(`${at}.${key}`, `'${key}' is not a field of the questions part`);
  const assumptions = value.assumptions;
  if (!Array.isArray(assumptions) || !assumptions.every((a) => isRecord(a) && isText(a.point) && isText(a.chose) && extraKeys(a, ['point', 'chose']).length === 0)) {
    report(`${at}.assumptions`, 'assumptions is a list of { point, chose }: what the intent left open, and what was chosen');
  }
  const open = value.openQuestions;
  if (!Array.isArray(open) || !open.every(isText)) report(`${at}.openQuestions`, 'openQuestions is a list of questions; an empty list says there are none');
  if (problems.length > 0) return { ok: false, problems };
  return {
    ok: true,
    part: {
      assumptions: (assumptions as Array<{ point: string; chose: string }>).map(({ point, chose }) => ({ point, chose })),
      openQuestions: [...(open as string[])],
    },
  };
}

 /**
  * Artifact paths are workspace-relative and stay inside it. The Vault and
  * every station resolve them against the workspace; an absolute path or a

@@ -434,79 +495,80 @@ export interface GraphBinding {
 /**
  * The task graph, bound to its run, or the problems that keep it from being
  * one. At M1 a graph schedules `build` and `review` tasks only. A build task
  * depends only on build tasks; a review task depends on at least one build
  * task and on nothing else; every build task is covered by a review task, so
  * no work reaches `integrate` unreviewed; and the dependencies are acyclic, so
  * the graph can finish.
  */
 export function parseGraph(value: unknown, bind: GraphBinding): { ok: true; graph: TaskGraph } | { ok: false; problems: RequestProblem[] } {
   const { problems, report } = collector();
   const raw = typeof value === 'object' && value !== null && 'tasks' in value ? value.tasks : undefined;
   if (!Array.isArray(raw) || raw.length === 0) {
     report('graph.tasks', 'missing', 'graph.tasks must hold at least one task');
     return { ok: false, problems };
   }
   const entries: readonly unknown[] = raw;
   const ids = new Set<string>();
   const stationOf = new Map<string, string>();
   entries.forEach((entry, i) => {
     const t = (typeof entry === 'object' && entry !== null ? entry : {}) as Record<string, unknown>;
     const at = `graph.tasks[${String(i)}]`;
     if (!isNonEmptyString(t.id)) report(`${at}.id`, 'empty', `${at}.id must be a non-empty string`);
+    else if (SEAT_TASK_IDS.includes(t.id as TaskId)) report(`${at}.id`, 'reserved', `${at}.id '${t.id}' is a drafting seat's id in run state; a graph task may not use it (R19)`);
     else if (ids.has(t.id)) report(`${at}.id`, 'duplicate', `${at}.id '${t.id}' is listed twice`);
     else {
       ids.add(t.id);
       if (typeof t.station === 'string') stationOf.set(t.id, t.station);
     }
     if (t.station !== 'build' && t.station !== 'review') {
       report(`${at}.station`, 'bad-station', `${at}.station must be 'build' or 'review'; M1 schedules tasks at no other station`);
     }
     if (!isNonEmptyString(t.role)) report(`${at}.role`, 'empty', `${at}.role must be a non-empty string`);
     if (!Array.isArray(t.dependsOn) || !t.dependsOn.every(isNonEmptyString)) {
       report(`${at}.dependsOn`, 'empty', `${at}.dependsOn must be a list of task ids`);
     }
     if (!Array.isArray(t.dependencySet) || t.dependencySet.length === 0 || !t.dependencySet.every(isNonEmptyString)) {
       report(`${at}.dependencySet`, 'missing', `${at}.dependencySet must name at least one glob`);
     }
   });
   if (problems.length > 0) return { ok: false, problems };
 
   const tasks = entries as ReadonlyArray<{ id: string; station: 'build' | 'review'; role: string; dependsOn: string[]; dependencySet: string[] }>;
   const reviewed = new Set<string>();
   tasks.forEach((t, i) => {
     const at = `graph.tasks[${String(i)}].dependsOn`;
     t.dependsOn.forEach((dep, j) => {
       const depStation = stationOf.get(dep);
       if (depStation === undefined) report(`${at}[${String(j)}]`, 'bad-dependency', `${at}[${String(j)}]: '${dep}' is not a task in the graph`);
       else if (depStation !== 'build') {
         report(`${at}[${String(j)}]`, 'bad-dependency', `${at}[${String(j)}]: '${dep}' is a ${depStation} task; tasks depend only on build tasks`);
       } else if (t.station === 'review') reviewed.add(dep);
     });
     if (t.station === 'review' && t.dependsOn.length === 0) {
       report(at, 'bad-dependency', `${at}: a review task must depend on the build task it reviews`);
     }
   });
   tasks.forEach((t, i) => {
     if (t.station === 'build' && !reviewed.has(t.id)) {
       report(`graph.tasks[${String(i)}]`, 'unreviewed', `graph.tasks[${String(i)}]: build task '${t.id}' is covered by no review task`);
     }
   });
   if (!tasks.some((t) => t.station === 'build')) report('graph.tasks', 'missing', 'graph.tasks must hold at least one build task');
   const cycle = findCycle(tasks);
   if (cycle !== undefined) report('graph.tasks', 'cycle', `graph.tasks: the dependencies cycle through ${cycle.join(' -> ')}`);
   if (problems.length > 0) return { ok: false, problems };
 
   const bound: Task[] = tasks.map((t) => ({
     id: t.id as TaskId,
     runId: bind.runId,
     station: t.station,
     role: t.role as RoleId,
     dependsOn: t.dependsOn.map((dep) => dep as TaskId),
     baseCommit: bind.baseCommit,
     dependencySet: [...t.dependencySet],
     worktreePath: bind.workspace,
     attempt: 1,
   }));
   const edges = bound.flatMap((task) => task.dependsOn.map((dep) => ({ from: dep, to: task.id })));
   return { ok: true, graph: { tasks: bound, edges } };
 }

+/**
+ * Writes each file's bytes at its path under `root`, replacing what is
+ * there, and refuses a path whose parent is a link: the runtime writes
+ * approved draft bytes into the trees it owns and nowhere through them (R19).
+ */
+export async function writeFiles(root: string, files: ReadonlyArray<{ readonly path: string; readonly bytes: Uint8Array }>): Promise<void> {
+  for (const file of files) {
+    await refuseLinkedParent(root, file.path, 'write');
+    const destination = join(root, ...file.path.split('/'));
+    await rm(destination, { recursive: true, force: true });
+    await mkdir(dirname(destination), { recursive: true });
+    await writeFile(destination, file.bytes);
+  }

+export async function filesIn(root: string): Promise<string[]> {

/**
 * R19's pure pieces: what a critic's result is read as, the two spec parts,
 * the request shape a drafting run takes, the worst case with its seats, and
 * the rates keyed by independence grade. The line itself is driven end to end
 * by the conformance registry's R19 assertions.
 */

import { StrictPolicyEngine, type PolicyDocument, type RoleId, type RunId, type TaskGraph, type TaskId } from '@olympus-ai/core';

  deriveOutcomes,
  isDraftableDirectory,

describe('a critic\'s result (D-A-DS-07)', () => {
  it('is a list of findings with no severity, and anything else is one finding', () => {
    expect(readCriticResult('{"findings": []}')).toEqual({ readable: true, findings: [] });
    expect(readCriticResult('```json\n{"findings": ["statement 2 is not testable"]}\n```')).toEqual({ readable: true, findings: ['statement 2 is not testable'] });
    for (const narrative of ['Looks good!', '{"findings": [], "severity": "low"}', '{"findings": [""]}', '{"findings": "none"}', '[]', '']) {
      const read = readCriticResult(narrative);
      expect(read.readable).toBe(false);
      expect(read.findings).toHaveLength(1);
    }
  });
});

describe('the spec parts R19 adds', () => {
  it('reads the run requirements, and refuses a test directory outside the tree or under the drafts', () => {
    const part = { testCommand: ['pnpm', 'test'], framework: 'vitest', testDirectory: 'test/acceptance/', publicInterface: 'add(a, b) from src/math.ts' };
    expect(parseRunRequirements(part, 'p')).toEqual({ ok: true, part: { ...part, testDirectory: 'test/acceptance' } });
    for (const testDirectory of ['../out', '/abs', '.', '.drafts/x', '.git/hooks', 'a\\b', '']) {
      expect(isDraftableDirectory(testDirectory)).toBe(false);
      expect(parseRunRequirements({ ...part, testDirectory }, 'p').ok).toBe(false);
    }
    expect(parseRunRequirements({ ...part, extra: 1 }, 'p').ok).toBe(false);
    expect(parseRunRequirements({ ...part, testCommand: [] }, 'p').ok).toBe(false);
  });

  it('reads the questions, an empty list of open questions saying there are none', () => {
    expect(parseQuestions({ assumptions: [{ point: 'p', chose: 'c' }], openQuestions: [] }, 'q')).toEqual({ ok: true, part: { assumptions: [{ point: 'p', chose: 'c' }], openQuestions: [] } });
    expect(parseQuestions({ assumptions: [{ point: 'p' }], openQuestions: [] }, 'q').ok).toBe(false);
    expect(parseQuestions({ assumptions: [], openQuestions: [''] }, 'q').ok).toBe(false);
  });
});

describe('a drafting run\'s request', () => {
  const base = { runId: 'r', baseCommit: 'c', requestedLevel: 1, workspace: process.cwd(), approvedCostUsd: null };
  const artifacts = { spec: ['spec.md'], acceptanceTests: ['t.md'], verificationManifest: 'v.json', taskGraph: 'g.json' };
  const codes = (a: unknown) => requestProblems({ ...base, artifacts: a }).map((p) => `${p.path}:${p.code}`);

  it('takes draft for the spec or the tests, explicitly, and never an empty list', () => {
    expect(codes({ ...artifacts, spec: 'draft' })).toEqual([]);
    expect(codes({ ...artifacts, spec: [] })).toEqual(['artifacts.spec:missing']);
  });

  it('names no parts for a drafted spec, needs the run requirements for drafted tests, and no supplied acceptance manifest beside them', () => {
    expect(codes({ ...artifacts, spec: 'draft', specParts: { security: 's.json' } })).toEqual(['artifacts.specParts.security:not-applicable']);
    expect(codes({ ...artifacts, acceptanceTests: 'draft' })).toEqual(['artifacts.specParts.runRequirements:missing']);
    expect(codes({ ...artifacts, acceptanceTests: 'draft', specParts: { runRequirements: 'r.json' } })).toEqual([]);
    expect(codes({ ...artifacts, spec: 'draft', acceptanceTests: 'draft' })).toEqual([]);
    expect(codes({ ...artifacts, acceptanceTests: 'draft', acceptanceManifest: 'a.json', specParts: { runRequirements: 'r.json' } })).toEqual(['artifacts.acceptanceManifest:not-applicable']);
  });
});

describe('the worst case with drafting seats (R19, D-P9-03)', () => {
  const scope = (stations: string[], maxCostUsd: number) => ({
    stations, writableGlobs: ['**'], tools: [], network: { egress: 'none' as const }, tier: 'standard' as const, tierByStation: {}, escalation: 'none' as const,
    autonomyCeiling: 2 as const, triggerKinds: ['human' as const], budget: { maxTokens: 1, maxCostUsd, maxWallClockMs: 1 },
  });
  const doc = {
    globalCap: 2, stationCaps: {}, approvals: {}, protectedPaths: [], concurrency: { maxParallelTasks: 1, maxConflictRetries: 0 },
    triggers: { enabled: ['human'], entryStation: { human: 'intake' }, taskTemplate: {}, maxAutonomy: { human: 2 }, minAuthorTrust: { human: 'owner' }, maxTriggerDepth: 2, budgetPerWindow: { runs: 1, windowMs: 1 } },
    roles: {
      builder: scope(['build'], 1), reviewer: scope(['review'], 1),
      'spec-drafter': scope(['spec'], 0.5), 'spec-critic': scope(['spec'], 0.25), 'test-drafter': scope(['test-design'], 0.5), 'test-critic': scope(['test-design'], 0.25),
    },
  } as unknown as PolicyDocument;
  const policy = new StrictPolicyEngine().resolvePolicy(doc);
  const graph: TaskGraph = {
    tasks: [
      { id: 'b' as TaskId, runId: 'r' as RunId, station: 'build', role: 'builder' as RoleId, dependsOn: [], baseCommit: 'c', dependencySet: ['**'], worktreePath: '', attempt: 1 },
      { id: 'v' as TaskId, runId: 'r' as RunId, station: 'review', role: 'reviewer' as RoleId, dependsOn: ['b' as TaskId], baseCommit: 'c', dependencySet: ['**'], worktreePath: '', attempt: 1 },
    ],
    edges: [],
  };

  it('counts every call each seat a drafted station holds may make, at its own budget, and none for a station not drafted', () => {
    const without = worstCaseCost(graph, policy);
    const spec = worstCaseCost(graph, policy, ['spec']);
    const both = worstCaseCost(graph, policy, ['spec', 'test-design']);
    // Three attempts, each with two retries: nine calls a seat.
    expect(spec.tasks.filter((t) => t.station === 'spec').map((t) => [t.task, t.calls, t.usd])).toEqual([['spec.drafter', 9, 4.5], ['spec.critic', 9, 2.25]]);
    expect(spec.usd).toBeCloseTo(without.usd + 6.75);
    expect(both.usd).toBeCloseTo(without.usd + 13.5);
  });
});

describe('rates keyed by independence grade (D-A-DS-06)', () => {
  const run = (id: string, reviewGrade: string, critiques: RunRecords['critiques'] = []): RunRecords => ({
    runId: id as RunId, ending: 'merged', requestedLevel: 2, stack: 's', startedAt: null, mergedAt: null, approvalsSpent: 0,
    tasks: [{ taskId: 't' as TaskId, station: 'build', status: 'passed', iterations: 1, attempts: [{ outcome: 'passed', claimMismatch: false }], firstModel: 'm', models: ['m'] }],
    decisions: [], usage: [], cost: null, reviewGrade, critiques,
  });

  it('keeps the false-done rate and the autonomy ratio per review grade, and critic findings per critic grade', () => {
    const o = deriveOutcomes([
      run('a', 'different-family', [{ grade: 'different-family', found: true }, { grade: 'different-family', found: false }]),
      run('b', 'same-model', [{ grade: 'different-model', found: false }]),
    ]);
    expect(Object.keys(o.safety.caughtFalseDoneByGrade)).toEqual(['different-family', 'same-model']);
    expect(Object.keys(o.throughput.autonomyRatioByGrade)).toEqual(['different-family', 'same-model']);
    expect(o.throughput.criticFindingsByGrade['different-family']).toMatchObject({ count: 1, sample: 2 });
    expect(o.throughput.criticFindingsByGrade['different-model']).toMatchObject({ count: 0, sample: 1 });
  });
});

 export async function runCli(argv: readonly string[], io: CliIo): Promise<ExitCode> {
   try {
     const { values, positionals } = parseArgs({
       args: [...argv],
       strict: false,
       allowPositionals: true,
       options: { url: { type: 'string' }, token: { type: 'string' }, help: { type: 'boolean' } },
     });
     const [command, ...rest] = positionals;
     if (values.help === true || command === undefined) {
       io.out(USAGE);
       return command === undefined && values.help !== true ? 2 : 0;
     }
     const t = target({ url: typeof values.url === 'string' ? values.url : undefined, token: typeof values.token === 'string' ? values.token : undefined }, io.env);
     // What remains once the global options are taken out: the command's own arguments.
     const own = stripGlobals(argv).slice(1);
     switch (command) {
       case 'create':
         return await create(io, t, own);
       case 'status': {
         const [runId = ''] = one(rest, ['<run id>']);
         return report(io, await call(io, t, 'GET', runPath(runId)));
       }
       case 'approve': {
-        const [runId = '', key = ''] = one(rest, ['<run id>', '<station:level>']);
-        const body: ApproveBody = { key: key as ApproveBody['key'] };
+        const { values: flags, positionals: named } = parseArgs({ args: own, strict: true, allowPositionals: true, options: { draft: { type: 'string' } } });
+        const [runId = '', key = ''] = one(named, ['<run id>', '<station:level>']);
+        const body: ApproveBody = { key: key as ApproveBody['key'], ...(flags.draft === undefined ? {} : { draft: flags.draft }) };
         return report(io, await call(io, t, 'POST', runPath(runId, 'approve'), body));
       }
+      case 'reject': {
+        const { values: flags, positionals: named } = parseArgs({
+          args: own,
+          strict: true,
+          allowPositionals: true,
+          options: { draft: { type: 'string' }, notes: { type: 'string' }, reason: { type: 'string' } },
+        });
+        const [runId = '', key = ''] = one(named, ['<run id>', '<station:level>']);
+        const body: RejectBody = {
+          key: key as RejectBody['key'],
+          draft: required(flags.draft, '--draft'),
+          ...(flags.notes === undefined ? {} : { notes: flags.notes }),
+          ...(flags.reason === undefined ? {} : { reason: flags.reason as NonNullable<RejectBody['reason']> }),
+        };
+        return report(io, await call(io, t, 'POST', runPath(runId, 'reject'), body));
+      }
       case 'cancel': {
         const [runId = ''] = one(rest, ['<run id>']);
         return report(io, await call(io, t, 'POST', runPath(runId, 'cancel')));
       }
       case 'events': {
         const [runId = ''] = one(rest, ['<run id>']);
         return await events(io, t, runId);
       }
       case 'report': {
         const [runId = ''] = one(rest, ['<run id>']);
         return report(io, await call(io, t, 'GET', runPath(runId, 'report')));
       }
       case 'outcomes':
         return await outcomes(io, t, own);
       default:
         throw new UsageError(`unknown command '${command}'`);
     }
   } catch (error) {
     if (error instanceof UsageError || (error instanceof TypeError && 'code' in error && String(error.code).startsWith('ERR_PARSE_ARGS'))) {
       io.err(error.message);
       io.err(USAGE);
       return 2;
     }
     io.err(`could not reach the API: ${error instanceof Error ? error.message : 'unknown error'}`);
     return 2;
   }
 }

===== packages/compiler/roles/spec-critic.md =====
# Spec critic

You read a drafted spec and say what is wrong with it. You did not write it,
and you change nothing: your working directory is read-only.

## What you work from

Your context holds the intent the spec was drafted from, the draft itself (its
four files, each under its path), and the rubric a person wrote for this check.
The repository is in your working directory, with the draft's files in it.

## What to check

Check the draft against the rubric. At the least:

- Can each statement of `spec.md` be checked by a test? Name any that cannot.
- Does the spec cover the intent, and nothing beyond it?
- Is the security part honest about the boundaries, data, rules, and secrets
  the change touches?
- Are the assumptions real choices, and is every point the intent left open
  either an assumption or an open question?
- Would a test writer who sees only the spec and `run-requirements.json` know
  how to call the public interface?

## What to say

Your final message is your result, and nothing else: one JSON object,

```
{"findings": ["...", "..."]}
```

Each finding is one problem, said so the drafter can fix it. Give no severity
and no praise. If you found nothing wrong, return `{"findings": []}`. Anything
other than that object is counted as a finding.

===== packages/compiler/roles/spec-drafter.md =====
# Spec drafter

You draft the spec for one run. The message you are given is your seat's id
and the attempt you are on. On a later attempt it also says why the last draft
went back.

## What you work from

Your context holds the intent: a person's statement of what they want. The
repository is in your working directory, so you can read how it is built,
tested, and laid out. It is a copy: nothing you change in it leaves your seat
except the four files below.

## What you write

Write exactly these four files in the one directory under `.drafts/` in your
working directory, which the runtime made for this run and left empty:

- `spec.md`: one shippable capability, in statements each of which a test can
  check. Say what the change does, and what it must not do. Cover the intent
  and nothing beyond it.
- `security.json`: the spec's security part. Either
  `{ "none": "<one line saying why the change touches no trust boundary>" }`,
  or `{ "trustBoundaries": [...], "sensitiveData": [...],
  "authorizationRules": [{ "id": "...", "rule": "..." }], "secrets": [...] }`,
  each list possibly empty. Name every authorization rule the change adds or
  relies on: each one will need a test that is refused by it.
- `run-requirements.json`: what the acceptance tests need to run, for a test
  writer who will never see this repository:
  `{ "testCommand": ["..."], "framework": "...", "testDirectory": "...",
  "publicInterface": "..." }`. `testDirectory` is where the framework finds
  acceptance tests, relative to the repository root. `publicInterface` says
  exactly what a test calls and how to reach it from a file in
  `testDirectory`: module paths, function or command names, inputs, outputs.
- `questions.json`: `{ "assumptions": [{ "point": "...", "chose": "..." }],
  "openQuestions": ["..."] }`. List each point the intent left undecided and
  what you chose. List as an open question anything you could not decide
  without guessing what the person wants.

## What you must not do

- Do not settle an open question yourself. A spec with an open question goes
  to a person; that is the right outcome, not a failure.
- Do not write tests, change the code, or write anywhere else in the tree.
  Nothing else you write is read.
- Do not reach for anything the session does not offer you.

## How you are judged

The runtime reads the four files, checks that each part parses, and records
the bytes it read. A critic checks the draft against a rubric a person wrote,
and a person or the policy decides whether it is approved. Nothing you say
decides it.

## What to say when you finish

Say what you drafted, and say plainly what you were unsure of. Do not describe
work you did not do.

===== packages/compiler/roles/test-critic.md =====
# Test critic

You read drafted acceptance tests and say what is wrong with them. You did not
write them, you change nothing, and like their author you see only the locked
spec: your working directory holds the spec and the draft, read-only.

## What you work from

Your context holds the locked spec, the draft (its test files and its
acceptance manifest, each under its path), and the rubric a person wrote for
this check.

## What to check

Check the draft against the rubric. At the least:

- Does each test check behavior the spec states, and only that?
- Does each test stay black-box: does it call only the public interface the
  spec's run requirements name, never an implementation detail?
- Do the tests cover the spec's cases, including each authorization rule the
  security part names, with a caller that rule refuses?
- Does each check declare honestly what it does before the change?

## What to say

Your final message is your result, and nothing else: one JSON object,

```
{"findings": ["...", "..."]}
```

Each finding is one problem, said so the drafter can fix it. Give no severity
and no praise. If you found nothing wrong, return `{"findings": []}`. Anything
other than that object is counted as a finding.

===== packages/compiler/roles/test-drafter.md =====
# Test drafter

You write the acceptance tests for one run, from its locked spec alone. The
message you are given is your seat's id and the attempt you are on. On a later
attempt it also says why the last draft went back.

## What you work from

Your working directory holds the locked spec and nothing else: its text, its
security part, and its `run-requirements.json`, and a directory under
`.drafts/` made for this run, which may already hold the spec's files. You cannot see the repository, and nothing you do
can reach it. That is deliberate: the tests judge the capability the spec
describes, not an implementation you have seen.

## What you write

- Test files under the `testDirectory` that `run-requirements.json` names,
  each written for its `framework` and calling only the `publicInterface` it
  describes. Each test checks behavior the spec states.
- `acceptance.json`, in the directory under `.drafts/`: `{ "checks": [...] }`,
  one check per test. An `acceptance` check names its test files in `tests`;
  a `behavioral` check runs a pinned `command` that names its test file. Each check declares
  `onBase`: `"fails"` where the behavior is absent before the change, which is
  what reproduces it, and `"passes"` where the test guards something that
  holds already. At least one required check declares `"fails"`. For each
  authorization rule `security.json` names, write a test in which a caller the
  rule refuses is refused, and name the rule in that check's `deniesRule`.

## What you must not do

- Do not test anything the spec does not state.
- Do not change the spec's files. Nothing outside the test directory and the
  manifest is read.
- Do not try to reach the repository or the network.

## How you are judged

The runtime runs your checks over the repository before any change is made,
and each must do what it declares there. If one does not, you learn its id,
its declaration, and its exit code or why it did not run, and nothing else. A
critic checks the tests against the spec and a rubric a person wrote. Nothing
you say decides it.

## What to say when you finish

Say which tests you wrote and what each checks. Do not describe work you did
not do.

 function deepFreeze(value: object): void {
   for (const inner of Object.values(value as Record<string, unknown>)) {
     if (typeof inner === 'object' && inner !== null) deepFreeze(inner);
@@ -206,3 +297,36 @@ export function contractTableProblems(table: StationContractTable): ContractProb
   }
   return problems;

+/**
+ * Every way the critic table breaks its rules, checked on the values as the
+ * station table is: a critic that can write, a critic granted anything its
+ * drafter is not beyond the draft and the rubric, or a `test-design` critic
+ * granted anything beyond the locked spec, the draft, and the rubric.
+ */
+export function criticContractProblems(table: CriticContractTable, drafters: StationContractTable = STATION_CONTRACTS): ContractProblem[] {
+  const problems: ContractProblem[] = [];
+  for (const station of DRAFT_STATIONS) {
+    const contract: unknown = Object.hasOwn(table, station) ? table[station] : undefined;
+    if (typeof contract !== 'object' || contract === null) {
+      problems.push({ station, message: 'the critic table has no contract for this station' });
+      continue;
+    }
+    const c = contract as Partial<Record<keyof CriticContract, unknown>>;
+    if (c.station !== station) problems.push({ station, message: `the critic contract is filed under '${station}' but names '${String(c.station)}'` });
+    const writes = c.writeBoundary as Partial<Record<keyof WriteBoundary, unknown>> | undefined;
+    if (!Array.isArray(writes?.workspaceGlobs) || writes.workspaceGlobs.length > 0) problems.push({ station, message: 'a critic writes nothing' });
+    if (writes?.vault !== 'never') problems.push({ station, message: 'the write boundary must deny the Vault' });
+    const context: readonly unknown[] = Array.isArray(c.allowedContext) ? c.allowedContext : [];
+    const drafter = drafters[station].allowedContext;
+    for (const grant of context) {
+      if (!drafter.includes(grant as ContextGrant) && grant !== 'draft' && grant !== 'rubric') {
+        problems.push({ station, message: `a critic is granted what its drafter is, the draft, and the rubric; not '${String(grant)}'` });
+      }
+    }
+    if (station === 'test-design' && !(context.length === TEST_CRITIC_CONTEXT.length && TEST_CRITIC_CONTEXT.every((g, i) => context[i] === g))) {
+      problems.push({ station, message: `the test-design critic may be granted the locked spec, the draft, and the rubric alone; it is granted [${context.map(String).join(', ')}]` });
+    }
+  }
+  return problems;
+}

+/** Whether a grant clears this exit: the key, unspent, and for a drafted exit the same draft and attempt (R19). */
+export function grantClears(grant: ApprovalGrant, key: ApprovalKey, draft: DraftRef | null): boolean {
+  if (grant.key !== key || grant.usedAt !== null) return false;
+  const named = grant.draft;
+  if (draft === null) return named === null;
+  return named !== null && named.sha256 === draft.sha256 && named.attempt === draft.attempt;

 /**

  * (I3). A move back up the line — `verify` returning a task to `build` — is a
  * rebuild, not an advance, and needs no approval. An advance needs the exit

  */

   }

+const GRADE_RANK: Readonly<Record<IndependenceGrade, number>> = { 'different-family': 0, 'different-model': 1, 'same-model': 2 };

+/**
+ * How independent two seats are, from the models the runtime resolved for
+ * them (D-A-DS-06). The family is the declared one, never inferred (I6); a
+ * model is the same model when its provider, name, and version all match.
+ */
+export function gradeOf(a: ModelIdentity, b: ModelIdentity): IndependenceGrade {
+  if (a.family !== b.family) return 'different-family';
+  return a.provider === b.provider && a.model === b.model && a.version === b.version ? 'same-model' : 'different-model';
+}
+
+/** The weaker of two grades: what a pair compared twice, or a seat compared with several authors, can claim. */
+export function weakerGrade(a: IndependenceGrade, b: IndependenceGrade): IndependenceGrade {
+  return GRADE_RANK[a] >= GRADE_RANK[b] ? a : b;
+}
+
+/** The weakest grade between `seat` and any of `others`. */
+export function gradeAgainst(seat: ModelIdentity, others: readonly ModelIdentity[]): IndependenceGrade {
+  return others.reduce<IndependenceGrade>((grade, other) => weakerGrade(grade, gradeOf(seat, other)), 'different-family');
+}

 /**
  * The next thing the line does, from committed state alone.
  *
  * `build` builds every task whose dependencies have passed and that has not,
  * up to `limits.maxParallelTasks` at once, in graph order (R4); `verify`
  * verifies one task at a time, every task
  * `build` left verifying; leaving `verify` goes back to `build` while any build
  * task has not passed, and on to `review` once all have. A task whose status is
  * `running` was in flight when the run stopped, and is run again.
  */
 export function nextStep(state: RunState, graph: TaskGraph, limits: StepLimits, contracts: StationContractTable = STATION_CONTRACTS): Step {
   if (!Number.isInteger(limits.maxParallelTasks) || limits.maxParallelTasks < 1) {
     throw new Error(`station machine: ${String(limits.maxParallelTasks)} tasks at once is not a positive integer; refused rather than building none`);
   }
   // A cancelled run does nothing more, whatever else its state holds (A-P9-01).
   if (state.cancelled !== null) {
     const { by, at } = state.cancelled;
     return {
       kind: 'refuse',
       refusal: { ok: false, reason: 'cancelled', cancelledBy: by, cancelledAt: at, message: `the run was cancelled by ${by} at ${at} and does not continue` },
     };
   }
   // A drive that ended in an error stopped the run without saying why in its state; the halt is that record (A-P9-02).
   if (state.halted !== null) {
     const { at, message } = state.halted;
     return { kind: 'refuse', refusal: { ok: false, reason: 'halted', haltedAt: at, message: `a drive of the run ended in an error at ${at} and the run does not continue: ${message}` } };
   }
   if (state.violations.length > 0) {
     return {
       kind: 'refuse',
       refusal: {
         ok: false,
         reason: 'violation',
         violations: [...state.violations],
         message: `the run has ${String(state.violations.length)} recorded integrity violation(s) and does not continue`,
       },
     };
   }
   const parked = graph.tasks.find((task) => statusOf(state, task) === 'parked');
   if (parked !== undefined) return { kind: 'refuse', refusal: parkRefusal(state, parked, limits, contracts) };
+  // A drafting seat parks like a task, and a person's rejection of a station's last draft parks the run (R19).
+  for (const station of DRAFT_STATIONS) {
+    for (const seat of ['drafter', 'critic'] as const) {
+      const task = seatTask(state, station, seat);
+      if (statusOf(state, task) === 'parked') return { kind: 'refuse', refusal: parkRefusal(state, task, limits, contracts) };
+    }
+    if (state.drafts[station]?.parked === true) return { kind: 'refuse', refusal: draftsRejected(station, contracts) };
+  }

     if (state.station === 'verify') {
       if (builds.every((task) => statusOf(state, task) === 'passed')) return { kind: 'exit', from: 'verify', to: 'review' };
       if (!builds.some((task) => BUILDABLE.has(statusOf(state, task) ?? 'pending') && depsPassed(state, task))) {
         throw new Error('station machine: leaving verify with build tasks unpassed, and none of them can be built; the graph cannot finish');
       }
       return { kind: 'exit', from: 'verify', to: 'build' };
     }

     if (to === null) throw new Error(`station machine: there is no station after ${state.station}`);
     return { kind: 'exit', from: state.station, to };

     }
     case 'verify': {
       const task = builds.find((t) => statusOf(state, t) === 'verifying');
       return task === undefined ? { kind: 'finish', station: 'verify' } : { kind: 'verify', task };
     }
     case 'review': {
       const task = reviews.find((t) => {
         const status = statusOf(state, t) ?? 'pending';
         return status === 'pending' || status === 'running';
       });
       return task === undefined ? { kind: 'finish', station: 'review' } : { kind: 'review', task };
     }

     case 'spec':
     case 'test-design':
     case 'plan':
     case 'integrate':
       return { kind: 'work', station: state.station };

       throw new Error(`station machine: M1 never enters ${state.station}`);

+/**
+ * A drafter's retry as its request's variable suffix: the typed parts of
+ * what sent its last draft back, and nothing else (D-A-DS-02). Never in the
+ * stable prefix or a role. At `test-design` the locked-spec-alone rule
+ * admits these parts and no other: a person's notes there are refused, since
+ * an approver who has read the repository would otherwise be a channel
+ * around the black box, and a test drafter learns of a base run only each
+ * check's id, declaration, and exit code or fixed cause.
+ */
+export function retrySuffix(station: DraftStation, attempt: number, parts: readonly RetryPart[]): string {
+  const lines = [`attempt ${String(attempt)}`];
+  for (const part of parts) {
+    switch (part.kind) {
+      case 'critic-findings':
+        lines.push('[critic-findings]', ...part.findings.map((f) => `- ${f}`));
+        break;
+      case 'runtime-problems':
+        lines.push('[runtime-problems]', ...part.problems.map((p) => `- ${p.path}: ${p.code}`));
+        break;
+      case 'base-refusal':
+        lines.push('[base-refusal]', ...part.checks.map((c) => `- ${c.checkId} declared ${c.declared ?? 'nothing'}: ${'exitCode' in c ? `exit ${String(c.exitCode)}` : c.cause}`));
+        break;
+      case 'rejection-reason':
+        lines.push('[rejection-reason]', part.reason);
+        break;
+      case 'notes':
+        if (station !== 'spec') throw new Error(`station machine: a ${station} retry carries no notes; a rejection there carries a reason from a fixed set`);
+        lines.push('[approver-notes]', part.notes);
+        break;
+    }
+  }
+  return lines.join('\n');
+}

+/**
+ * The context a critic seat is given: the offered parts its contract grants,
+ * in the contract's order, and nothing else (R19). The contract is checked
+ * again here rather than trusted, as `grantedContext` checks a station's: a
+ * `test-design` critic granted anything beyond the locked spec, the draft,
+ * and the rubric throws before anything is assembled.
+ */
+export function grantedCriticContext(contract: CriticContract, offered: Partial<Record<ContextGrant, string>>): ContextPart[] {
+  const problems = criticContractProblems({ ...CRITIC_CONTRACTS, [contract.station]: contract });
+  if (problems.length > 0) throw new Error(`station machine: ${problems.map((p) => `${p.station}: ${p.message}`).join('; ')}`);
+  const parts: ContextPart[] = [];
+  for (const grant of contract.allowedContext) {
+    const text = Object.hasOwn(offered, grant) ? offered[grant] : undefined;
+    if (typeof text === 'string') parts.push({ grant, text });
+  }
+  return parts;
+}

+/**
+ * Seats a test drafter against every model the build driver can resolve for
+ * the run (R19, R9's `test-design` and `build` row). A drafter of a builder's
+ * family writes the tests its own family is judged by: at L3 it is refused,
+ * as a same-family reviewer is; below L3 it is seated and the floor's
+ * `drafter-independent-of-builder` condition fails, so its draft goes to a
+ * person. The grade is the weakest against any builder model.
+ */
+export function seatTestDrafter(
+  drafter: ModelIdentity, builders: readonly ModelIdentity[], level: AutonomyLevel,
+): { readonly ok: true; readonly grade: IndependenceGrade } | StationRefusal {

+    return {
+      ok: false,

+      family: drafter.family,
+      message: `the test drafter shares the model family '${drafter.family}' with a model the build driver can resolve, and L3 does not seat it`,
+    };
+  }
+  return { ok: true, grade };

+/** The stations a drafter may be seated at (R19). */

+  | 'independent-critic-clean'
+  | 'base-run-passed'
+  | 'drafter-independent-of-builder';
+
+/**
+ * Why a person rejected a test draft: a fixed set and nothing else, since an
+ * approver who has read the repository would otherwise be a channel around
+ * the black box (D-A-DS-02).
+ */
+export type RejectionReason = 'not-black-box' | 'does-not-follow-spec' | 'not-runnable' | 'misses-spec-cases';
+
+/**
+ * What a check did on the base that refused a test draft, as the next
+ * attempt learns it: the check's id, its declaration, and either its exit
+ * code or a cause from a fixed set. Never its output, which carries the
+ * repository (I7, D-A-DS-01).
+ */
+export type BaseRefusalPart =
+  | { readonly checkId: string; readonly declared: 'fails' | 'passes' | null; readonly exitCode: number }
+  | { readonly checkId: string; readonly declared: 'fails' | 'passes' | null; readonly cause: 'did-not-start' | 'suite-short' };
+
+/**
+ * A typed part of a drafter's retry, carried in the request's variable
+ * suffix and never in its stable prefix or role (D-A-DS-02). The runtime's
+ * own problems with a draft are paths and codes; `notes` exist at `spec`
+ * alone, and a `test-design` rejection carries a reason and no text.
+ */
+export type RetryPart =
+  | { readonly kind: 'critic-findings'; readonly findings: readonly string[] }
+  | { readonly kind: 'runtime-problems'; readonly problems: ReadonlyArray<{ readonly path: string; readonly code: string }> }
+  | { readonly kind: 'base-refusal'; readonly checks: readonly BaseRefusalPart[] }
+  | { readonly kind: 'rejection-reason'; readonly reason: RejectionReason }
+  | { readonly kind: 'notes'; readonly notes: string };
+
 /**
  * One declared check that did not do on the base what the locked manifest
  * declared (D-A-BF-01). `contrary`: it exited 0 where it was declared to
  * fail, or non-zero where it was declared to pass. `no-result`: it did not
  * start, which is never counted as a failure. `suite-count`: the base's
  * suites could not be counted, or counted below `expectedSuiteCount`, so the
  * runner may never have loaded the test (I5).
  */
 export interface BaseMismatch {
   checkId: string;
   /** `null` for a security check, which declares nothing: it runs over the base for its findings, and one with none readable there is `no-result` (D-R17-01). */
   declared: 'fails' | 'passes' | null;
   /** The check's own exit code on the base, or null when it produced no result. */
   exitCode: number | null;
   cause: 'contrary' | 'no-result' | 'suite-count';
 }
 
-/** A part the runtime requires of every spec, whatever its template; the security part is the only one today. */
-export type SpecPart = 'security';
+/**
+ * A part of a spec, each a JSON file locked with it. The runtime requires the
+ * security part of every spec (R17). A drafted spec carries all three: what
+ * the acceptance checks need to run, and its assumptions and open questions
+ * (R19); a supplied spec may.
+ */
+export type SpecPart = 'security' | 'run-requirements' | 'questions';
 
 /** A locked artifact whose bytes no longer match the lock manifest (I3). */
 export interface TamperedPath { path: string; expected: string; actual: string; }
 
 /**
  * Why a task stopped being attempted. `iterations-exhausted`: its gate failed
  * on every one of the contract's `maxIterations` builds. `retries-exhausted`:
  * its driver or sandbox failed more times than the contract's `retry.max`.
  * `starts-exhausted`: the driver was invoked more times than any uninterrupted
  * run of the station could invoke it, which is a run being replayed into the
  * same attempt rather than making progress (A-P4-06). `conflicts-exhausted`:
  * a sibling's accepted change collided with the task's own more times than the
  * policy's `concurrency.maxConflictRetries` allows (A-R4-02).
+ * `drafts-rejected`: a person rejected a drafting station's last allowed
+ * draft, which parks the run terminally (R19, D-A-DS-02).
  */
-export type ParkCause = 'iterations-exhausted' | 'retries-exhausted' | 'starts-exhausted' | 'conflicts-exhausted';
+export type ParkCause = 'iterations-exhausted' | 'retries-exhausted' | 'starts-exhausted' | 'conflicts-exhausted' | 'drafts-rejected';
 
 /**
  * A refusal to advance. `reason` is a closed set and selects the payload a
  * machine reads; `message` says the same thing to a person. One string never
  * does both jobs, so a consumer reads fields and never parses prose.
  *
  * The three approval and seat arms are I4 and I6 made refusals: a `blocked`
  * approval cell, a `human-required` cell with no recorded grant, and, at L3
  * only, a reviewer whose model family is an author's. None of them is a
  * warning the run may continue past.
  *
  * `cancelled` is a human's stop, recorded in run state: the run does not
  * continue, and a resume reads the record and refuses (A-P9-01).
  */
 export type StationRefusal =
   | { ok: false; reason: 'gate-failed'; failed: FailedCheck[]; message: string }
   | { ok: false; reason: 'lock-tamper'; tampered: TamperedPath[]; message: string }
   /**
    * The declared checks run over the base at the `test-design` exit did not
    * do what the manifest declared (D-A-BF-01). `baseRun` is the record the
    * runtime wrote of that run; `mismatched` is never empty.
    */
   | { ok: false; reason: 'base-mismatch'; mismatched: [BaseMismatch, ...BaseMismatch[]]; baseRun: VaultRef; message: string }
   /** A part every spec must carry was not admitted, refused at the `spec` exit (R17). */
   | { ok: false; reason: 'spec-part-missing'; part: SpecPart; message: string }
   /**
    * Authorization rules the spec's security part names with no locked
    * acceptance or behavioral check naming them, refused at the `test-design`
    * exit (R17). Never empty.
    */
   | { ok: false; reason: 'denial-check-missing'; rules: [string, ...string[]]; message: string }
   | { ok: false; reason: 'violation'; violations: VaultRef[]; message: string }
   | { ok: false; reason: 'unsafe-above-l1'; components: string[]; message: string }
   | { ok: false; reason: 'capability-missing'; station: StationId; capability: DriverCapability; message: string }
   | { ok: false; reason: 'approval-blocked'; key: ApprovalKey; message: string }
   /**
    * `escalations`: what raised the exit to a human beyond its own floor and
    * the policy cell — each protected path touched and each tamper finding —
    * so the person approving is told why (A-I1a-07). Empty when nothing did.
    */
   | { ok: false; reason: 'approval-required'; key: ApprovalKey; escalations: string[]; message: string }
   | { ok: false; reason: 'same-family-reviewer'; task: TaskId; family: ModelFamily; message: string }
-  | { ok: false; reason: 'parked'; task: TaskId; cause: Exclude<ParkCause, 'conflicts-exhausted'>; limit: number; message: string }
+  /** At L3, a test drafter of a family the build driver can resolve: refused as a same-family reviewer is (R19, R9). */
+  | { ok: false; reason: 'same-family-drafter'; station: 'test-design'; family: ModelFamily; message: string }
+  | { ok: false; reason: 'parked'; task: TaskId; cause: Exclude<ParkCause, 'conflicts-exhausted' | 'drafts-rejected'>; limit: number; message: string }
+  /**
+   * A drafting station's last allowed draft rejected by a person. A draft has
+   * no graph task, so the arm names the station (R19).
+   */
+  | { ok: false; reason: 'parked'; task: null; station: DraftStation; cause: 'drafts-rejected'; limit: number; message: string }
   /**
    * A conflict past the policy's bound: the siblings and the paths of the last
    * one, read from run state, so the refusal names both sides (A-R4-02).
    */
   | {
     ok: false; reason: 'parked'; task: TaskId; cause: 'conflicts-exhausted'; limit: number;
     conflictsWith: readonly [TaskId, ...TaskId[]]; paths: readonly [string, ...string[]]; message: string;
   }
   | { ok: false; reason: 'cancelled'; cancelledBy: string; cancelledAt: string; message: string }
   | { ok: false; reason: 'halted'; haltedAt: string; message: string };
 
 /**
  * `spends` names the approval grant the advance consumes, or null when the
  * exit needed none. The line marks that grant used in the same commit as the
  * station move, so one human approval crosses one exit (A-P4-04).
  */
 export type StationTransition = { ok: true; next: StationId; spends: ApprovalKey | null } | StationRefusal;
+
+/**
+ * A critic's seat at a drafting station (R19): the drafter's grants, the
+ * recorded draft, and the rubric, and nothing writable. One call per draft,
+ * retried on a driver failure like any seat.
+ */
+export interface CriticContract {
+  readonly station: DraftStation;
+  readonly requires: DriverCapability[];
+  readonly allowedContext: ContextGrant[];
+  readonly writeBoundary: WriteBoundary & { readonly workspaceGlobs: [] };
+  readonly retry: { readonly max: number; readonly backoffMs: number };
+}

+function isModel(m: unknown): boolean {

+function isDraftRef(d: unknown): boolean {

+function isPathCodes(value: unknown): boolean {

+/**
+ * The hash a draft is named by: the SHA-256 of the canonical list of each
+ * file's path and hash, sorted by path (R19). Exported for the runtime, which
+ * computes it from the bytes it read, so the two cannot drift.
+ */
+export function draftDigest(files: ReadonlyArray<{ readonly path: string; readonly sha256: string }>): string {
+  const canonical = [...files].sort((a, b) => (a.path < b.path ? -1 : a.path > b.path ? 1 : 0)).map((f) => [f.path, f.sha256]);
+  return sha256(new TextEncoder().encode(JSON.stringify(canonical)));
+}
+
+/** R19: a draft as the runtime read it, whole, with its hash the hash of its files and each file's the hash of its bytes. */
+function draftProblem(r: unknown): string | undefined {
+  if (!isRecord(r) || !hasString(r, 'runId')) return 'it names no run';
+  if (r.collectedBy !== 'runtime') return "collectedBy is not 'runtime'";
+  if (typeof r.station !== 'string' || !DRAFT_STATIONS.has(r.station)) return 'it names no drafting station';
+  if (!Number.isInteger(r.attempt) || (r.attempt as number) < 1) return 'attempt is not a positive whole number';
+  if (!Array.isArray(r.files) || r.files.length === 0) return 'it holds no file';
+  const seen = new Set<string>();
+  for (const file of r.files as unknown[]) {
+    if (!isRecord(file) || !hasString(file, 'path') || !hasString(file, 'sha256') || !hasString(file, 'base64')) return 'a file carries no path, hash, and bytes';
+    const { path, sha256: hash, base64 } = file as { path: string; sha256: string; base64: string };
+    if (seen.has(path)) return `${path} is listed twice`;
+    seen.add(path);
+    if (sha256(Buffer.from(base64, 'base64')) !== hash) return `${path}'s hash is not the hash of its bytes`;
+  }
+  if (r.sha256 !== draftDigest(r.files as Array<{ path: string; sha256: string }>)) return 'its hash is not the hash of its files';
+  if (!Array.isArray(r.discarded) || !r.discarded.every((d) => typeof d === 'string')) return 'its discarded paths are not a list';
+  if (!isPathCodes(r.problems)) return 'its problems are not paths and codes';
+  if (!isModel(r.model)) return 'it names no model the drafter ran on';
+  if (!isRoleVersion(r.role)) return 'it names no role and version the drafter ran under';
+  return undefined;
+}
+
+/** R19: a critic's result, whole: a binding result is of another family, and an unreadable one carries a finding (I5). */
+function criticProblem(r: unknown): string | undefined {
+  if (!isRecord(r) || !hasString(r, 'runId')) return 'it names no run';
+  if (r.collectedBy !== 'runtime') return "collectedBy is not 'runtime'";
+  if (typeof r.station !== 'string' || !DRAFT_STATIONS.has(r.station)) return 'it names no drafting station';
+  if (!Number.isInteger(r.attempt) || (r.attempt as number) < 1) return 'attempt is not a positive whole number';
+  if (typeof r.draft !== 'string' || !SHA256_HEX.test(r.draft)) return 'it names no draft hash';
+  if (!isModel(r.drafter) || !isModel(r.critic)) return 'it names no drafter and critic model';
+  if (r.grade !== 'different-family' && r.grade !== 'different-model' && r.grade !== 'same-model') return 'its grade is not a grade';
+  if (r.binding !== (r.grade === 'different-family')) return 'only a critic of another family than the drafter is binding (D-A-DS-06)';
+  if (typeof r.readable !== 'boolean') return 'it does not say whether the result was readable';
+  if (!Array.isArray(r.findings) || !r.findings.every((f) => typeof f === 'string' && f !== '')) return 'its findings are not a list of text';

+  return undefined;

+  return undefined;

 /**
  * Stores a record under its own SHA-256. Writing the same bytes twice for the
